LG1871	991	(They go to guard some corpse—the flag tops are draped with black muslin.)
LG1871	992	I hear the violoncello, ('tis the young man's heart's complaint;)
LG1871	993	I hear the key'd cornet—it glides quickly in through my ears;
LG1871	994	It shakes mad-sweet pangs through my belly and breast.
LG1871	995	I hear the chorus—it is a grand opera;
LG1871	996	Ah, this indeed is music! This suits me.
LG1871	997	A tenor large and fresh as the creation fills me;
LG1871	998	The orbic flex of his mouth is pouring and filling me full.
LG1871	999	I hear the train'd soprano—(what work, with hers, is this?)
LG1871	1000	The orchestra whirls me wider than Uranus flies;
LG1871	1001	It wrenches such ardors from me, I did not know I possess'd them;
LG1871	1002	It sails me—I dab with bare feet—they are lick'd by the indolent waves;
LG1871	1003	I am exposed, cut by bitter and angry hail—I lose my breath,
LG1871	1004	Steep'd amid honey'd morphine, my windpipe throt-tledthrottled in fakes of death;
LG1871	1005	At length let up again to feel the puzzle of puzzles,
LG1871	1006	And that we call BEING.
LG1871	1007	To be, in any form—what is that?
LG1871	1008	(Round and round we go, all of us, and ever come back thither;)
LG1871	1009	If nothing lay more develop'd, the quahaug in its cal-louscallous shell were enough.
LG1871	1010	Mine is no callous shell;
LG1871	1011	I have instant conductors all over me, whether I pass or stop;
LG1871	1012	They seize every object and lead it harmlessly through me.
LG1871	1013	I merely stir, press, feel with my fingers, and am happy;
LG1871	1014	To touch my person to some one else's is about as much as I can stand.
LG1871	1015	Is this then a touch? quivering me to a new iden-tityidentity,
LG1871	1016	Flames and ether making a rush for my veins,
LG1871	1017	Treacherous tip of me reaching and crowding to help them,
LG1871	1018	My flesh and blood playing out lightning to strike what is hardly different from myself;
LG1871	1019	On all sides prurient provokers stiffening my limbs,
LG1871	1020	Straining the udder of my heart for its withheld drip,
LG1871	1021	Behaving licentious toward me, taking no denial,
LG1871	1022	Depriving me of my best, as for a purpose,
LG1871	1023	Unbuttoning my clothes, holding me by the bare waist,
LG1871	1024	Deluding my confusion with the calm of the sunlight and pasture-fields,
LG1871	1025	Immodestly sliding the fellow-senses away,
LG1871	1026	They bribed to swap off with touch, and go and graze at the edges of me;
LG1871	1027	No consideration, no regard for my draining strength or my anger;
LG1871	1028	Fetching the rest of the herd around to enjoy them a while,
LG1871	1029	Then all uniting to stand on a headland and worry me.
LG1871	1030	The sentries desert every other part of me;
LG1871	1031	They have left me helpless to a red marauder;
LG1871	1032	They all come to the headland, to witness and assist against me.
LG1871	1033	I am given up by traitors;
LG1871	1034	I talk wildly—I have lost my wits—I and nobody else am the greatest traitor;
LG1871	1035	I went myself first to the headland—my own hands car-riedcarried me there.
LG1871	1036	You villain touch! what are you doing? My breath is tight in its throat;
LG1871	1037	Unclench your floodgates! you are too much for me.
LG1871	1038	Blind, loving, wrestling touch! sheath'd, hooded, sharp tooth'd touch!
LG1871	1039	Did it make you ache so, leaving me?
LG1871	1040	Parting, track'd by arriving—perpetual payment of perpetual loan;
LG1871	1041	Rich, showering rain, and recompense richer after-wardafterward.
LG1871	1042	Sprouts take and accumulate—stand by the curb prolific and vital:
LG1871	1043	Landscapes, projected, masculine, full-sized and golden.
LG1871	1044	All truths wait in all things;
LG1871	1045	They neither hasten their own delivery, nor resist it;
LG1871	1046	They do not need the obstetric forceps of the surgeon;
LG1871	1047	The insignificant is as big to me as any;
LG1871	1048	(What is less or more than a touch?)
LG1871	1049	Logic and sermons never convince;
LG1871	1050	The damp of the night drives deeper into my soul.
LG1871	1051	Only what proves itself to every man and woman is so;
LG1871	1052	Only what nobody denies is so.
LG1871	1053	A minute and a drop of me settle my brain;
LG1871	1054	I believe the soggy clods shall become lovers and lamps,
LG1871	1055	And a compend of compends is the meat of a man or woman,
LG1871	1056	And a summit and flower there is the feeling they have for each other,
LG1871	1057	And they are to branch boundlessly out of that lesson until it becomes omnific,
LG1871	1058	And until every one shall delight us, and we them.
LG1871	1059	I believe a leaf of grass is no less than the journey-workjourney-work of the stars,
LG1871	1060	And the pismire is equally perfect, and a grain of sand, and the egg of the wren,
LG1871	1061	And the tree-toad is a chef-d'uvre for the highest,
LG1871	1062	And the running blackberry would adorn the parlors of heaven,
LG1871	1063	And the narrowest hinge in my hand puts to scorn all machinery,
LG1871	1064	And the cow crunching with depress'd head surpasses any statue,
LG1871	1065	And a mouse is miracle enough to stagger sextillions of infidels,
LG1871	1066	And I could come every afternoon of my life to look at the farmer's girl boiling her iron tea-kettle and baking short-cake.
LG1871	1067	I find I incorporate gneiss, coal, long-threaded moss, fruits, grains, esculent roots,
LG1871	1068	And am stucco'd with quadrupeds and birds all over,
LG1871	1069	And have distanced what is behind me for good rea-sonsreasons,
LG1871	1070	And call anything close again, when I desire it.
LG1871	1071	In vain the speeding or shyness;
LG1871	1072	In vain the plutonic rocks send their old heat against my approach;
LG1871	1073	In vain the mastodon retreats beneath its own powder'd bones;

LG1871	1080	I follow quickly, I ascend to the nest in the fissure of the cliff.
LG1871	1081	I think I could turn and live with animals, they are so placid and self-contain'd;
LG1871	1082	I stand and look at them long and long.
LG1871	1083	They do not sweat and whine about their condition;
LG1871	1084	They do not lie awake in the dark and weep for their sins;
LG1871	1085	They do not make me sick discussing their duty to God;
LG1871	1086	Not one is dissatisfied—not one is demented with the mania of owning things;
LG1871	1087	Not one kneels to another, nor to his kind that lived thousands of years ago;
LG1871	1088	Not one is respectable or industrious over the whole earth.
LG1871	1089	So they show their relations to me, and I accept them;
LG1871	1090	They bring me tokens of myself—they evince them plainly in their possession.
LG1871	1091	I wonder where they get those tokens:
LG1871	1092	Did I pass that way huge times ago, and negligently drop them?

LG1871	1272	The hundred and fifty are dumb yet at Alamo;)
LG1871	1273	'Tis the tale of the murder in cold blood of four hun-dredhundred and twelve young men.
LG1871	1274	Retreating, they had form'd in a hollow square, with their baggage for breastworks;
LG1871	1275	Nine hundred lives out of the surrounding enemy's, nine times their number, was the price they took in advance;
LG1871	1276	Their colonel was wounded and their ammunition gone;
LG1871	1277	They treated for an honorable capitulation, receiv'd writing and seal, gave up their arms, and march'd back prisoners of war.
LG1871	1278	They were the glory of the race of rangers;

LG1871	1283	The second First-day morning they were brought out in squads, and massacred—it was beautiful early summer;
LG1871	1284	The work commenced about five o'clock, and was over by eight.
LG1871	1285	None obey'd the command to kneel;
LG1871	1286	Some made a mad and helpless rush—some stood stark and straight;

LG1871	1294	That is the tale of the murder of the four hundred and twelve young men.
LG1871	1295	Would you hear of an old-fashion'd sea-fight?
LG1871	1296	Would you learn who won by the light of the moon and stars?
LG1871	1297	List to the story as my grandmother's father, the sailor, told it to me.
LG1871	1298	Our foe was no skulk in his ship, I tell you, (said he;)
LG1871	1299	His was the surly English pluck—and there is no tougher or truer, and never was, and never will be;
LG1871	1300	Along the lower'd eve he came, horribly raking us.
LG1871	1301	We closed with him—the yards entangled—the can-noncannon touch'd;
LG1871	1302	My captain lash'd fast with his own hands.
LG1871	1303	We had receiv'd some eighteen pound shots under the water;
LG1871	1304	On our lower-gun-deck two large pieces had burst at the first fire, killing all around, and blowing up overhead.
LG1871	1305	Fighting at sun-down, fighting at dark;
LG1871	1306	Ten o'clock at night, the full moon well up, our leaks on the gain, and five feet of water reported;
LG1871	1307	The master-at-arms loosing the prisoners confined in the after-hold, to give them a chance for them-selvesthemselves.

LG1871	1311	The other asks if we demand quarter?

LG1871	1863	I knew a man, a common farmer—the father of five sons;

LG1871	2349	NOT heaving from my ribb'd breast only;

LG1871	2382	AND now, gentlemen,
LG1871	2383	A word I give to remain in your memories and minds,

LG1871	2476	I SAW in Louisiana a live-oak growing,

LG1871	2499	THIS moment yearning and thoughtful, sitting alone,

LG1871	2561	Nor literary success, nor intellect—nor book for the book-shelf;
LG1871	2562	Only a few carols, vibrating through the air, I leave,
LG1871	2563	For comrades and lovers.
LG1871	2564	A LEAF for hand in hand!
LG1871	2565	You natural persons old and young!
LG1871	2566	You on the Mississippi, and on all the branches and bayous of the Mississippi!
LG1871	2567	You friendly boatmen and mechanics! You roughs!
LG1871	2568	You twain! And all processions moving along the streets!
LG1871	2569	I wish to infuse myself among you till I see it com-moncommon for you to walk hand in hand!
LG1871	2570	TO the East and to the West;
LG1871	2571	To the man of the Seaside State, and of Pennsylvania,
LG1871	2572	To the Kanadian of the North—to the Southerner I love;
LG1871	2573	These, with perfect trust, to depict you as myself—the germs are in all men;
LG1871	2574	I believe the main purport of These States is to found a superb friendship, exalt, previously unknown,
LG1871	2575	Because I perceive it waits, and has been always wait-ingwaiting, latent in all men.
LG1871	2576	EARTH! my likeness!
LG1871	2577	Though you look so impassive, ample spheric there,
LG1871	2578	I now suspect that is not all;
LG1871	2579	I now suspect there is something fierce in you, eligible to burst forth;
LG1871	2580	For an athlete is enamour'd of me—and I of him;
LG1871	2581	But toward him there is something fierce and terrible in me, eligible to burst forth,
LG1871	2582	I dare not tell it in words—not even in these songs.
LG1871	2583	I DREAM'D in a dream, I saw a city invincible to the attacks of the whole of the rest of the earth;
LG1871	2584	I dream'd that was the new City of Friends;
LG1871	2585	Nothing was greater there than the quality of robust love—it led the rest;
LG1871	2586	It was seen every hour in the actions of the men of that city,
LG1871	2587	And in all their looks and words.
LG1871	2588	FAST-ANCHOR'D, eternal, O love! O woman I love!
LG1871	2589	O bride! O wife! more resistless than I can tell, the thought of you!
LG1871	2590	—Then separate, as disembodied, or another born,
LG1871	2591	Ethereal, the last athletic reality, my consolation;
LG1871	2592	I ascend—I float in the regions of your love, O man,
LG1871	2593	O sharer of my roving life.
LG1871	2594	SOMETIMES with one I love, I fill myself with rage, for fear I effuse unreturn'd love;
LG1871	2595	But now I think there is no unreturn'd love—the pay is certain, one way or another;
LG1871	2596	(I loved a certain person ardently, and my love was not return'd;
LG1871	2597	Yet out of that, I have written these songs.)
LG1871	2598	THAT shadow, my likeness, that goes to and fro, seek-ingseeking a livelihood, chattering, chaffering;
LG1871	2599	How often I find myself standing and looking at it where it flits;
LG1871	2600	How often I question and doubt whether that is really me;
LG1871	2601	—But in these, and among my lovers, and caroling my songs,
LG1871	2602	O I never doubt whether that is really me.
LG1871	2603	AMONG the men and women, the multitude,
LG1871	2604	I perceive one picking me out by secret and divine signs,
LG1871	2605	Acknowledging none else—not parent, wife, husband, brother, child, any nearer than I am;
LG1871	2606	Some are baffled—But that one is not—that one knows me.
LG1871	2607	Ah, lover and perfect equal!
LG1871	2608	I meant that you should discover me so, by my faint indirections;
LG1871	2609	And I, when I meet you, mean to discover you by the like in you.
LG1871	2610	O BOY of the West!
LG1871	2611	To you many things to absorb, I teach, to help you become eleve of mine:
LG1871	2612	Yet if blood like mine circle not in your veins;
LG1871	2613	If you be not silently selected by lovers, and do not silently select lovers,
LG1871	2614	Of what use is it that you seek to become eleve of mine?
LG1871	2615	O YOU whom I often and silently come where you are, that I may be with you;
LG1871	2616	As I walk by your side, or sit near, or remain in the same room with you,
LG1871	2617	Little you know the subtle electric fire that for your sake is playing within me.
LG1871	2618	FULL of life, now, compact, visible,
LG1871	2619	I, forty years old the Eighty-third Year of The States,
LG1871	2620	To one a century hence, or any number of centuries hence,
LG1871	2621	To you, yet unborn, these, seeking you.
LG1871	2622	When you read these, I, that was visible, am become invisible;
LG1871	2623	Now it is you, compact, visible, realizing my poems, seeking me;
LG1871	2624	Fancying how happy you were, if I could be with you, and become your comrade;
LG1871	2625	Be it as if I were with you. (Be not too certain but I am now with you.)
LG1871	2626	O TAKE my hand, Walt Whitman!
LG1871	2627	Such gliding wonders! such sights and sounds!
LG1871	2628	Such join'd unended links, each hook'd to the next!
LG1871	2629	Each answering all—each sharing the earth with all.
LG1871	2630	What widens within you, Walt Whitman?
LG1871	2631	What waves and soils exuding?
LG1871	2632	What climes? what persons and lands are here?
LG1871	2633	Who are the infants? some playing, some slumbering?
LG1871	2634	Who are the girls? who are the married women?
LG1871	2635	Who are the groups of old men going slowly with their arms about each other's necks?
LG1871	2636	What rivers are these? what forests and fruits are these?
LG1871	2637	What are the mountains call'd that rise so high in the mists?
LG1871	2638	What myriads of dwellings are they, fill'd with dwellers?
LG1871	2639	Within me latitude widens, longitude lengthens;
LG1871	2640	Asia, Africa, Europe, are to the east—America is pro-videdprovided for in the west;
LG1871	2641	Banding the bulge of the earth winds the hot equator,
LG1871	2642	Curiously north and south turn the axis-ends;
LG1871	2643	Within me is the longest day—the sun wheels in slant-ingslanting rings—it does not set for months;
LG1871	2644	Stretch'd in due time within me the midnight sun just rises above the horizon, and sinks again;
LG1871	2645	Within me zones, seas, cataracts, plants, volcanoes, groups,
LG1871	2646	Malaysia, Polynesia, and the great West Indian islands.
LG1871	2647	What do you hear, Walt Whitman?
LG1871	2648	I hear the workman singing, and the farmer's wife singing;
LG1871	2649	I hear in the distance the sounds of children, and of animals early in the day;
LG1871	2650	I hear quick rifle-cracks from the riflemen of East Ten-nesseeTennessee and Kentucky, hunting on hills;

LG1871	2674	What do you see, Walt Whitman?
LG1871	2675	Who are they you salute, and that one after another salute you?

LG1871	2868	(You will come forward in due time to my side.)
LG1871	2869	My spirit has pass'd in compassion and determina-tiondetermination around the whole earth;

LG1871	2871	I think some divine rapport has equalized me with them.

LG1871	2873	I think I have blown with you, O winds;
LG1871	2874	O waters, I have finger'd every shore with you.
LG1871	2875	I have run through what any river or strait of the globe has run through;

LG1871	2878	What cities the light or warmth penetrates, I penetrate those cities myself;
LG1871	2879	All islands to which birds wing their way, I wing my way myself.

LG1871	2882	To remain after me in sight forever,

LG1871	3113	How beggarly appear arguments before a defiant deed!

LG1871	3125	A sterile landscape covers the ore—there is as good as the best, for all the forbidding appearance;

LG1871	3218	Her shape arises,
LG1871	3219	She, less guarded than ever, yet more guarded than ever;
LG1871	3220	The gross and soil'd she moves among do not make her gross and soil'd;

LG1871	3234	AFOOT and light-hearted, I take to the open road,
LG1871	3235	Healthy, free, the world before me,
LG1871	3236	The long brown path before me, leading wherever I choose.
LG1871	3237	Henceforth I ask not good-fortune—I myself am good-fortunegood-fortune;
LG1871	3238	Henceforth I whimper no more, postpone no more, need nothing,
LG1871	3239	Strong and content, I travel the open road.
LG1871	3240	The earth—that is sufficient;
LG1871	3241	I do not want the constellations any nearer;
LG1871	3242	I know they are very well where they are;
LG1871	3243	I know they suffice for those who belong to them.
LG1871	3244	(Still here I carry my old delicious burdens;
LG1871	3245	I carry them, men and women—I carry them with me wherever I go;
LG1871	3246	I swear it is impossible for me to get rid of them;
LG1871	3247	I am fill'd with them, and I will fill them in return.)
LG1871	3248	You road I enter upon and look around! I believe you are not all that is here;
LG1871	3249	I believe that much unseen is also here.
LG1871	3250	Here the profound lesson of reception, neither prefer-encepreference or denial;
LG1871	3251	The black with his woolly head, the felon, the diseas'd, the illiterate person, are not denied;
LG1871	3252	The birth, the hasting after the physician, the beggar's tramp, the drunkard's stagger, the laughing party of mechanics,
LG1871	3253	The escaped youth, the rich person's carriage, the fop, the eloping couple,
LG1871	3254	The early market-man, the hearse, the moving of fur-niturefurniture into the town, the return back from the town,
LG1871	3255	They pass—I also pass—anything passes—none can be interdicted;
LG1871	3256	None but are accepted—none but are dear to me:
LG1871	3257	You air that serves me with breath to speak!
LG1871	3258	You objects that call from diffusion my meanings, and give them shape!
LG1871	3259	You light that wraps me and all things in delicate equable showers!
LG1871	3260	You paths worn in the irregular hollows by the road-sidesroadsides!
LG1871	3261	I think you are latent with unseen existences—you are so dear to me.
LG1871	3262	You flagg'd walks of the cities! you strong curbs at the edges!
LG1871	3263	You ferries! you planks and posts of wharves! you timber-lined sides! you distant ships!
LG1871	3264	You rows of houses! you window-pierc'd facades! you roofs!

LG1871	3266	You windows whose transparent shells might expose so much!
LG1871	3267	You doors and ascending steps! you arches!
LG1871	3268	You gray stones of interminable pavements! you trod-dentrodden crossings!
LG1871	3269	From all that has been near you, I believe you have im-partedimparted to yourselves, and now would impart the same secretly to me;
LG1871	3270	From the living and the dead I think you have peopled your impassive surfaces, and the spirits thereof would be evident and amicable with me.
LG1871	3271	The earth expanding right hand and left hand,
LG1871	3272	The picture alive, every part in its best light,
LG1871	3273	The music falling in where it is wanted, and stopping where it is not wanted,
LG1871	3274	The cheerful voice of the public road—the gay fresh sentiment of the road.
LG1871	3275	O highway I travel! O public road! do you say to me, Do not leave me?
LG1871	3276	Do you say, Venture not? If you leave me, you are lost?
LG1871	3277	Do you say, I am already prepared—I am well-beaten and undenied—adhere to me?
LG1871	3278	O public road! I say back, I am not afraid to leave you—yet I love you;
LG1871	3279	You express me better than I can express myself;
LG1871	3280	You shall be more to me than my poem.
LG1871	3281	I think heroic deeds were all conceiv'd in the open air, and all great poems also;
LG1871	3282	I think I could stop here myself, and do miracles;
LG1871	3283	(My judgments, thoughts, I henceforth try by the open air, the road:)
LG1871	3284	I think whatever I shall meet on the road I shall like, and whoever beholds me shall like me;
LG1871	3285	I think whoever I see must be happy.
LG1871	3286	From this hour, freedom!
LG1871	3287	From this hour I ordain myself loos'd of limits and imaginary lines,
LG1871	3288	Going where I list, my own master, total and absolute,
LG1871	3289	Listening to others, and considering well what they say,
LG1871	3290	Pausing, searching, receiving, contemplating,
LG1871	3291	Gently, but with undeniable will, divesting myself of the holds that would hold me.
LG1871	3292	I inhale great draughts of space;
LG1871	3293	The east and the west are mine, and the north and the south are mine.
LG1871	3294	I am larger, better than I thought,
LG1871	3295	I did not know I held so much goodness.
LG1871	3296	All seems beautiful to me;
LG1871	3297	I can repeat over to men and women, You have done such good to me, I would do the same to you.
LG1871	3298	I will recruit for myself and you as I go;
LG1871	3299	I will scatter myself among men and women as I go;
LG1871	3300	I will toss the new gladness and roughness among them;
LG1871	3301	Whoever denies me, it shall not trouble me;
LG1871	3302	Whoever accepts me, he or she shall be blessed, and shall bless me.
LG1871	3303	Now if a thousand perfect men were to appear, it would not amaze me;
LG1871	3304	Now if a thousand beautiful forms of women appear'd, it would not astonish me.
LG1871	3305	Now I see the secret of the making of the best per-sonspersons,
LG1871	3306	It is to grow in the open air, and to eat and sleep with the earth.
LG1871	3307	Here a great personal deed has room;
LG1871	3308	A great deed seizes upon the hearts of the whole race of men,
LG1871	3309	Its effusion of strength and will overwhelms law, and mocks all authority and all argument against it.
LG1871	3310	Here is the test of wisdom;
LG1871	3311	Wisdom is not finally tested in schools;
LG1871	3312	Wisdom cannot be pass'd from one having it, to an-otheranother not having it;
LG1871	3313	Wisdom is of the Soul, is not susceptible of proof, is its own proof,
LG1871	3314	Applies to all stages and objects and qualities, and is content,
LG1871	3315	Is the certainty of the reality and immortality of things, and the excellence of things;
LG1871	3316	Something there is in the float of the sight of things. that provokes it out of the Soul.
LG1871	3317	Now I rexamine philosophies and religions,
LG1871	3318	They may prove well in lecture-rooms, yet not prove at all under the spacious clouds, and along the landscape and flowing currents.
LG1871	3319	Here is realization;
LG1871	3320	Here is a man tallied—he realizes here what he has in him;
LG1871	3321	The past, the future, majesty, love—if they are vacant of you, you are vacant of them.
LG1871	3322	Only the kernel of every object nourishes;
LG1871	3323	Where is he who tears off the husks for you and me?
LG1871	3324	Where is he that undoes stratagems and envelopes for you and me?
LG1871	3325	Here is adhesiveness—it is not previously fashion'd— it is apropos;
LG1871	3326	Do you know what it is, as you pass, to be loved by strangers?
LG1871	3327	Do you know the talk of those turning eye-balls?
LG1871	3328	Here is the efflux of the Soul;
LG1871	3329	The efflux of the Soul comes from within, through em-bower'dembower'd gates, ever provoking questions:
LG1871	3330	These yearnings, why are they? These thoughts in the darkness, why are they?
LG1871	3331	Why are there men and women that while they are nigh me, the sun-light expands my blood!
LG1871	3332	Why, when they leave me, do my pennants of joy sink flat and lank?
LG1871	3333	Why are there trees I never walk under, but large and melodious thoughts descend upon me?
LG1871	3334	(I think they hang there winter and summer on those trees, and always drop fruit as I pass;)
LG1871	3335	What is it I interchange so suddenly with strangers?
LG1871	3336	What with some driver, as I ride on the seat by his side?
LG1871	3337	What with some fisherman, drawing his seine by the shore, as I walk by, and pause?

LG1871	3339	The efflux of the Soul is happiness—here is happi-nesshappiness;
LG1871	3340	I think it pervades the open air, waiting at all times;
LG1871	3341	Now it flows unto us—we are rightly charged.
LG1871	3342	Here rises the fluid and attaching character;
LG1871	3343	The fluid and attaching character is the freshness and sweetness of man and woman;
LG1871	3344	(The herbs of the morning sprout no fresher and sweeter every day out of the roots of themselves, than it sprouts fresh and sweet continually out of itself.)
LG1871	3345	Toward the fluid and attaching character exudes the sweat of the love of young and old;
LG1871	3346	From it falls distill'd the charm that mocks beauty and attainments;
LG1871	3347	Toward it heaves the shuddering longing ache of contact.
LG1871	3348	Allons! whoever you are, come travel with me!
LG1871	3349	Traveling with me, you find what never tires.
LG1871	3350	The earth never tires;
LG1871	3351	The earth is rude, silent, incomprehensible at first— Nature is rude and incomprehensible at first;
LG1871	3352	Be not discouraged—keep on—there are divine things, well envelop'd;
LG1871	3353	I swear to you there are divine things more beautiful than words can tell.
LG1871	3354	Allons! we must not stop here!
LG1871	3355	However sweet these laid-up stores—however conve-nientconvenient this dwelling, we cannot remain here;
LG1871	3356	However shelter'd this port, and however calm these waters, we must not anchor here;

LG1871	3358	Allons! the inducements shall be greater;
LG1871	3359	We will sail pathless and wild seas;
LG1871	3360	We will go where winds blow, waves dash, and the Yankee clipper speeds by under full sail.
LG1871	3361	Allons! with power, liberty, the earth, the elements!
LG1871	3362	Health, defiance, gayety, self-esteem, curiosity;
LG1871	3363	Allons! from all formules!
LG1871	3364	From your formules, O bat-eyed and materialistic priests!
LG1871	3365	The stale cadaver blocks up the passage—the burial waits no longer.

LG1871	3374	Listen! I will be honest with you;
LG1871	3375	I do not offer the old smooth prizes, but offer rough new prizes;
LG1871	3376	These are the days that must happen to you:
LG1871	3377	You shall not heap up what is call'd riches,

LG1871	3383	Allons! after the GREAT COMPANIONS! and to belong to them!

LG1871	3387	Enjoyers of calms of seas, and storms of seas,

LG1871	3389	Habitus of many distant countries, habitus of far-distantfar-distant dwellings,

LG1871	3395	Journeyers as with companions, namely, their own diverse phases,

LG1871	3400	Old age, calm, expanded, broad with the haughty breadth of the universe,
LG1871	3401	Old age, flowing free with the delicious near-by freedom of death.
LG1871	3402	Allons! to that which is endless, as it was begin-ninglessbeginningless,
LG1871	3403	To undergo much, tramps of days, rests of nights,
LG1871	3404	To merge all in the travel they tend to, and the days and nights they tend to,
LG1871	3405	Again to merge them in the start of superior journeys;
LG1871	3406	To see nothing anywhere but what you may reach it and pass it,
LG1871	3407	To conceive no time, however distant, but what you may reach it and pass it,
LG1871	3408	To look up or down no road but it stretches and waits for you—however long, but it stretches and waits for you;
LG1871	3409	To see no being, not God's or any, but you also go thither,
LG1871	3410	To see no possession but you may possess it—enjoying all without labor or purchase—abstracting the feast, yet not abstracting one particle of it;
LG1871	3411	To take the best of the farmer's farm and the rich man's elegant villa, and the chaste blessings of the well-marriedwell-married couple, and the fruits of orchards and flowers of gardens,

LG1871	3413	To carry buildings and streets with you afterward wherever you go,

LG1871	3415	To take your lovers on the road with you, for all that you leave them behind you,
LG1871	3416	To know the universe itself as a road—as many roads— as roads for traveling souls.
LG1871	3417	The Soul travels;
LG1871	3418	The body does not travel as much as the soul;
LG1871	3419	The body has just as great a work as the soul, and parts away at last for the journeys of the soul.
LG1871	3420	All parts away for the progress of souls;
LG1871	3421	All religion, all solid things, arts, governments,—all that was or is apparent upon this globe or any globe, falls into niches and corners before the procession of Souls along the grand roads of the universe.
LG1871	3422	Of the progress of the souls of men and women along the grand roads of the universe, all other progress is the needed emblem and sustenance.
LG1871	3423	Forever alive, forever forward,
LG1871	3424	Stately, solemn, sad, withdrawn, baffled, mad, turbulent, feeble, dissatisfied,
LG1871	3425	Desperate, proud, fond, sick, accepted by men, rejected by men,
LG1871	3426	They go! they go! I know that they go, but I know not where they go;
LG1871	3427	But I know that they go toward the best—toward some-thingsomething great.
LG1871	3428	Allons! whoever you are! come forth!
LG1871	3429	You must not stay sleeping and dallying there in the house, though you built it, or though it has been built for you.
LG1871	3430	Allons! out of the dark confinement!
LG1871	3431	It is useless to protest—I know all, and expose it.
LG1871	3432	Behold, through you as bad as the rest,
LG1871	3433	Through the laughter, dancing, dining, supping, of people,
LG1871	3434	Inside of dresses and ornaments, inside of those wash'd and trimm'd faces,
LG1871	3435	Behold a secret silent loathing and despair.
LG1871	3436	No husband, no wife, no friend, trusted to hear the confession;
LG1871	3437	Another self, a duplicate of every one, skulking and hiding it goes,
LG1871	3438	Formless and wordless through the streets of the cities, polite and bland in the parlors,
LG1871	3439	In the cars of rail-roads, in steamboats, in the public assembly,
LG1871	3440	Home to the houses of men and women, at the table, in the bed-room, everywhere,
LG1871	3441	Smartly attired, countenance smiling, form upright, death under the breast-bones, hell under the skull-bones,
LG1871	3442	Under the broadcloth and gloves, under the ribbons and artificial flowers,
LG1871	3443	Keeping fair with the customs, speaking not a syllable of itself,
LG1871	3444	Speaking of anything else, but never of itself.
LG1871	3445	Allons! through struggles and wars!
LG1871	3446	The goal that was named cannot be countermanded.
LG1871	3447	Have the past struggles succeeded?
LG1871	3448	What has succeeded? yourself? your nation? nature?
LG1871	3449	Now understand me well—It is provided in the essence of things, that from any fruition of success, no matter what, shall come forth something to make a greater struggle necessary.
LG1871	3450	My call is the call of battle—I nourish active rebel-lionrebellion;
LG1871	3451	He going with me must go well arm'd;
LG1871	3452	He going with me goes often with spare diet, poverty, angry enemies, desertions.
LG1871	3453	Allons! the road is before us!
LG1871	3454	It is safe—I have tried it—my own feet have tried it well.
LG1871	3455	Allons! be not detain'd!
LG1871	3456	Let the paper remain on the desk unwritten, and the book on the shelf unopen'd!
LG1871	3457	Let the tools remain in the workshop! let the money remain unearn'd!
LG1871	3458	Let the school stand! mind not the cry of the teacher!
LG1871	3459	Let the preacher preach in his pulpit! let the lawyer plead in the court, and the judge expound the law.
LG1871	3460	Mon enfant! I give you my hand!
LG1871	3461	I give you my love, more precious than money,
LG1871	3462	I give you myself, before preaching or law;
LG1871	3463	Will you give me yourself? will you come travel with me?
LG1871	3464	Shall we stick by each other as long as we live?
LG1871	3465	I SIT and look out upon all the sorrows of the world, and upon all oppression and shame;
LG1871	3466	I hear secret convulsive sobs from young men, at an-guishanguish with themselves, remorseful after deeds done;
LG1871	3467	I see, in low life, the mother misused by her children, dying, neglected, gaunt, desperate;
LG1871	3468	I see the wife misused by her husband—I see the treacherous seducer of young women;
LG1871	3469	I mark the ranklings of jealousy and unrequited love, attempted to be hid—I see these sights on the earth;
LG1871	3470	I see the workings of battle, pestilence, tyranny—I see martyrs and prisoners;
LG1871	3471	I observe a famine at sea—I observe the sailors casting lots who shall be kill'd, to preserve the lives of the rest;
LG1871	3472	I observe the slights and degradations cast by arrogant persons upon laborers, the poor, and upon ne-groesnegroes, and the like;
LG1871	3473	All these—All the meanness and agony without end, I sitting, look out upon,
LG1871	3474	See, hear, and am silent.
LG1871	3475	ME imperturbe, standing at ease in Nature,
LG1871	3476	Master of all, or mistress of all—aplomb in the midst of irrational things,
LG1871	3477	Imbued as they—passive, receptive, silent as they,
LG1871	3478	Finding my occupation, poverty, notoriety, foibles, crimes, less important than I thought;
LG1871	3479	Me private, or public, or menial, or solitary—all these subordinate, (I am eternally equal with the best —I am not subordinate;)
LG1871	3480	Me toward the Mexican Sea, or in the Mannahatta, or the Tennessee, or far north, or inland,
LG1871	3481	A river man, or a man of the woods, or of any farm-life of These States, or of the coast, or the lakes, or Kanada,
LG1871	3482	Me, wherever my life is lived, O to be self-balanced for contingencies!
LG1871	3483	O to confront night, storms, hunger, ridicule, accidents, rebuffs, as the trees and animals do.
LG1871	3484	As I lay with my head in your lap, Camerado,
LG1871	3485	The confession I made I resume—what I said to you and the open air I resume:
LG1871	3486	I know I am restless, and make others so;
LG1871	3487	I know my words are weapons, full of danger, full of death;
LG1871	3488	(Indeed I am myself the real soldier:
LG1871	3489	It is not he, there, with his bayonet, and not the red-stripedred-striped artilleryman;)
LG1871	3490	For I confront peace, security, and all the settled laws, to unsettle them;
LG1871	3491	I am more resolute because all have denied me, than I could ever have been had all accepted me;
LG1871	3492	I heed not, and have never heeded, either experience, cautions, majorities, nor ridicule;
LG1871	3493	And the threat of what is call'd hell is little or nothing to me;
LG1871	3494	And the lure of what is call'd heaven is little or nothing to me;
LG1871	3495	…Dear camerado! I confess I have urged you onward with me, and still urge you, without the least idea what is our destination,
LG1871	3496	Or whether we shall be victorious, or utterly quell'd and defeated.
LG1871	3497	FLOOD-TIDE below me! I watch you face to face;
LG1871	3498	Clouds of the west! sun there half an hour high! I see you also face to face.
LG1871	3499	Crowds of men and women attired in the usual cos-tumescostumes! how curious you are to me!
LG1871	3500	On the ferry-boats, the hundreds and hundreds that cross, returning home, are more curious to me than you suppose;
LG1871	3501	And you that shall cross from shore to shore years hence, are more to me, and more in my medita-tionsmeditations, than you might suppose.
LG1871	3502	The impalpable sustenance of me from all things, at all hours of the day;
LG1871	3503	The simple, compact, well-join'd scheme—myself disin-tegrateddisintegrated, every one disintegrated, yet part of the scheme;
LG1871	3504	The similitudes of the past, and those of the future;

LG1871	3508	The certainty of others—the life, love, sight, hearing of others.
LG1871	3509	Others will enter the gates of the ferry, and cross from shore to shore;
LG1871	3510	Others will watch the run of the flood-tide;
LG1871	3511	Others will see the shipping of Manhattan north and west, and the heights of Brooklyn to the south and east;

LG1871	3519	Just as you feel when you look on the river and sky, so I felt;
LG1871	3520	Just as any of you is one of a living crowd, I was one of a crowd;

LG1871	3524	I too many and many a time cross'd the river, the sun half an hour high;
LG1871	3525	I watched the Twelfth-month sea-gulls—I saw them high in the air, floating with motionless wings, oscillating their bodies,

LG1871	3527	I saw the slow-wheeling circles, and the gradual edging toward the south.
LG1871	3528	I too saw the reflection of the summer sky in the water,
LG1871	3529	Had my eyes dazzled by the shimmering track of beams,

LG1871	3550	I loved well the stately and rapid river;

LG1871	3563	I too had receiv'd identity by my Body;
LG1871	3564	That I was, I knew was of my body—and what I should be, I knew I should be of my body.

LG1871	3570	I am he who knew what it was to be evil;
LG1871	3571	I too knitted the old knot of contrariety,
LG1871	3572	Blabb'd, blush'd, resented, lied, stole, grudg'd,

LG1871	3586	Closer yet I approach you;
LG1871	3587	What thought you have of me, I had as much of you —I laid in my stores in advance;

LG1871	3644	WITH antecedents;

LG1871	3665	Its sun, and its again, all swing around us.
LG1871	3666	As for me, (torn, stormy, even as I, amid these vehe-mentvehement days,)

LG1871	3685	Now list to my morning's romanza—I tell the signs of the Answerer;
LG1871	3686	To the cities and farms I sing, as they spread in the sunshine before me.
LG1871	3687	A young man comes to me bearing a message from his brother;
LG1871	3688	How shall the young man know the whether and when of his brother?
LG1871	3689	Tell him to send me the signs.
LG1871	3690	And I stand before the young man face to face, and take his right hand in my left hand, and his left hand in my right hand,

LG1871	3692	Him all wait for—him all yield up to—his word is decisive and final,

LG1871	3695	Beautiful women, the haughtiest nations, laws, the landscape, people, animals,

LG1871	3702	He puts things in their attitudes;
LG1871	3703	He puts to-day out of himself, with plasticity and love;
LG1871	3704	He places his own city, times, reminiscences, parents, brothers and sisters, associations, employment, politics, so that the rest never shame them after-wardafterward, nor assume to command them.
LG1871	3705	He is the answerer;
LG1871	3706	What can be answer'd he answers—and what cannot be answer'd, he shows how it cannot be answer'd.
LG1871	3707	A man is a summons and challenge;
LG1871	3708	(It is vain to skulk—Do you hear that mocking and laughter? Do you hear the ironical echoes?)
LG1871	3709	Books, friendships, philosophers, priests, action, plea-surepleasure, pride, beat up and down, seeking to give satisfaction;
LG1871	3710	He indicates the satisfaction, and indicates them that beat up and down also.
LG1871	3711	Whichever the sex, whatever the season or place, he may go freshly and gently and safely, by day or by night;
LG1871	3712	He has the pass-key of hearts—to Him the response of the prying of hands on the knobs.
LG1871	3713	His welcome is universal—the flow of beauty is not more welcome or universal than he is;
LG1871	3714	The person he favors by day, or sleeps with at night, is blessed.
LG1871	3715	Every existence has its idiom—everything has an idiom and tongue;
LG1871	3716	He resolves all tongues into his own, and bestows it upon men, and any man translates, and any man translates himself also;
LG1871	3717	One part does not counteract another part—he is the joiner—he sees how they join.
LG1871	3718	He says indifferently and alike, How are you, friend? to the President at his levee,
LG1871	3719	And he says, Good-day, my brother! to Cudge that hoes in the sugar-field,
LG1871	3720	And both understand him, and know that his speech is right.
LG1871	3721	He walks with perfect ease in the Capitol,
LG1871	3722	He walks among the Congress, and one Representative says to another, Here is our equal, appearing and new.
LG1871	3723	Then the mechanics take him for a mechanic,
LG1871	3724	And the soldiers suppose him to be a soldier, and the sailors that he has follow'd the sea,
LG1871	3725	And the authors take him for an author, and the artists for an artist,
LG1871	3726	And the laborers perceive he could labor with them and love them;
LG1871	3727	No matter what the work is, that he is the one to fol-lowfollow it, or has follow'd it,
LG1871	3728	No matter what the nation, that he might find his brothers and sisters there,
LG1871	3729	The English believe he comes of their English stock,
LG1871	3730	A Jew to the Jew he seems—a Russ to the Russ—usual and near, removed from none.
LG1871	3731	Whoever he looks at in the traveler's coffee-house claims him,
LG1871	3732	The Italian or Frenchman is sure, and the German is sure, and the Spaniard is sure, and the island Cuban is sure;

LG1871	3736	They are not vile any more—they hardly know them-selvesthemselves, they are so grown.
LG1871	3737	THE indications, and tally of time;
LG1871	3738	Perfect sanity shows the master among philosophs;
LG1871	3739	Time, always without flaw, indicates itself in parts;
LG1871	3740	What always indicates the poet, is the crowd of the pleasant company of singers, and their words;
LG1871	3741	The words of the singers are the hours or minutes of the light or dark—but the words of the maker of poems are the general light and dark;
LG1871	3742	The maker of poems settles justice, reality, immor-talityimmortality,
LG1871	3743	His insight and power encircle things and the human race,
LG1871	3744	He is the glory and extract thus far, of things, and of the human race.
LG1871	3745	The singers do not beget—only the POET begets;
LG1871	3746	The singers are welcom'd, understood, appear often enough—but rare has the day been, likewise the spot, of the birth of the maker of poems, the Answerer,
LG1871	3747	(Not every century, or every five centuries, has con-tain'dcontain'd such a day, for all its names.)
LG1871	3748	The singers of successive hours of centuries may have ostensible names, but the name of each of them is one of the singers,
LG1871	3749	The name of each is, eye-singer, ear-singer, head-singerhead-singer, sweet-singer, echo-singer, parlor-singer, love-singer, or something else.
LG1871	3750	All this time, and at all times, wait the words of true poems;
LG1871	3751	The words of true poems do not merely please,
LG1871	3752	The true poets are not followers of beauty, but the august masters of beauty;
LG1871	3753	The greatness of sons is the exuding of the greatness of mothers and fathers,
LG1871	3754	The words of poems are the tuft and final applause of science.
LG1871	3755	Divine instinct, breadth of vision, the law of reason, health, rudeness of body, withdrawnness,
LG1871	3756	Gayety, sun-tan, air-sweetness—such are some of the words of poems.
LG1871	3757	The sailor and traveler underlie the maker of poems, the answerer;
LG1871	3758	The builder, geometer, chemist, anatomist, phrenolo-gistphrenologist, artist—all these underlie the maker of poems, the answerer.
LG1871	3759	The words of the true poems give you more than poems,
LG1871	3760	They give you to form for yourself, poems, religions, politics, war, peace, behavior, histories, essays, romances, and everything else,
LG1871	3761	They balance ranks, colors, races, creeds, and the sexes,
LG1871	3762	They do not seek beauty—they are sought,
LG1871	3763	Forever touching them, or close upon them, follows beauty, longing, fain, love-sick.
LG1871	3764	They prepare for death—yet are they not the finish, but rather the outset,
LG1871	3765	They bring none to his or her terminus, or to be con-tentcontent and full;
LG1871	3766	Whom they take, they take into space, to behold the birth of stars, to learn one of the meanings,
LG1871	3767	To launch off with absolute faith—to sweep through the ceaseless rings, and never be quiet again.
LG1871	3768	POETS to come! orators, singers, musicians to come!
LG1871	3769	Not to-day is to justify me, and answer what I am for;
LG1871	3770	But you, a new brood, native, athletic, continental, greater than before known,
LG1871	3771	Arouse! Arouse—for you must justify me—you must answer.
LG1871	3772	I myself but write one or two indicative words for the future,
LG1871	3773	I but advance a moment, only to wheel and hurry back in the darkness.
LG1871	3774	I am a man who, sauntering along, without fully stop-pingstopping, turns a casual look upon you, and then averts his face,
LG1871	3775	Leaving it to you to prove and define it,
LG1871	3776	Expecting the main things from you.
LG1871	3777	I HEAR America singing, the varied carols I hear;
LG1871	3778	Those of mechanics—each one singing his, as it should be, blithe and strong;
LG1871	3779	The carpenter singing his, as he measures his plank or beam,
LG1871	3780	The mason singing his, as he makes ready for work, or leaves off work;
LG1871	3781	The boatman singing what belongs to him in his boat— the deck-hand singing on the steamboat deck;
LG1871	3782	The shoemaker singing as he sits on his bench—the hatter singing as he stands;
LG1871	3783	The wood-cutter's song—the ploughboy's, on his way in the morning, or at the noon intermission, or at sundown;
LG1871	3784	The delicious singing of the mother—or of the young wife at work—or of the girl sewing or washing— Each singing what belongs to her, and to none else;
LG1871	3785	The day what belongs to the day—At night, the party of young fellows, robust, friendly,
LG1871	3786	Singing, with open mouths, their strong melodious songs.
LG1871	3787	BY the City Dead-House, by the gate,
LG1871	3788	As idly sauntering, wending my way from the clangor,
LG1871	3789	I curious pause—for lo! an outcast form, a poor dead prostitute brought;
LG1871	3790	Her corpse they deposit unclaim'd—it lies on the damp brick pavement;
LG1871	3791	The divine woman, her body—I see the Body—I look on it alone,
LG1871	3792	That house once full of passion and beauty—all else I notice not;
LG1871	3793	Nor stillness so cold, nor running water from faucet, nor odors morbific impress me;
LG1871	3794	But the house alone—that wondrous house—that deli-catedelicate fair house—that ruin!
LG1871	3795	That immortal house, more than all the rows of dwell-ingsdwellings ever built!
LG1871	3796	Or white-domed Capitol itself, with majestic figure sur-mountedsurmounted—or all the old high-spired cathedrals;
LG1871	3797	That little house alone, more than them all—poor, des-peratedesperate house!
LG1871	3798	Fair, fearful wreck! tenement of a Soul! itself a Soul!
LG1871	3799	Unclaim'd, avoided house! take one breath from my tremulous lips;
LG1871	3800	Take one tear, dropt aside as I go, for thought of you,
LG1871	3801	Dead house of love; house of madness and sin, crum-bledcrumbled! crush'd!
LG1871	3802	House of life—erewhile talking and laughing—but ah, poor house! dead, even then;
LG1871	3803	Months, years, an echoing, garnish'd house—but dead, dead, dead.
LG1871	3804	T HROUGH the ample open door of the peaceful country barn,
LG1871	3805	A sun-lit pasture field, with cattle and horses feeding;
LG1871	3806	And haze, and vista, and the far horizon, fading away.
LG1871	3807	COME closer to me;
LG1871	3808	Push close, my lovers, and take the best I possess!
LG1871	3809	Yield closer and closer, and give me the best you pos-sesspossess.
LG1871	3810	This is unfinished business with me—How is it with you?
LG1871	3811	(I was chill'd with the cold types, cylinder, wet paper between us.)
LG1871	3812	Male and Female!
LG1871	3813	I pass so poorly with paper and types, I must pass with the contact of bodies and souls.
LG1871	3814	American masses!
LG1871	3815	I do not thank you for liking me as I am, and liking the touch of me—I know that it is good for you to do so.
LG1871	3816	This is the carol of occupations;
LG1871	3817	In the labor of engines and trades, and the labor of fields, I find the developments,
LG1871	3818	And find the eternal meanings.
LG1871	3819	Workmen and Workwomen!
LG1871	3820	Were all educations, practical and ornamental, well dis-play'ddisplay'd out of me, what would it amount to?
LG1871	3821	Were I as the head teacher, charitable proprietor, wise statesman, what would it amount to?
LG1871	3822	Were I to you as the boss employing and paying you, would that satisfy you?
LG1871	3823	The learn'd, virtuous, benevolent, and the usual terms;
LG1871	3824	A man like me, and never the usual terms.
LG1871	3825	Neither a servant nor a master am I;
LG1871	3826	I take no sooner a large price than a small price—I will have my own, whoever enjoys me;
LG1871	3827	I will be even with you, and you shall be even with me.
LG1871	3828	If you stand at work in a shop, I stand as nigh as the nighest in the same shop;
LG1871	3829	If you bestow gifts on your brother or dearest friend, I demand as good as your brother or dearest friend;
LG1871	3830	If your lover, husband, wife, is welcome by day or night, I must be personally as welcome;
LG1871	3831	If you become degraded, criminal, ill, then I become so for your sake;
LG1871	3832	If you remember your foolish and outlaw'd deeds, do you think I cannot remember my own foolish and outlaw'd deeds?
LG1871	3833	If you carouse at the table, I carouse at the opposite side of the table;
LG1871	3834	If you meet some stranger in the streets, and love him or her—why I often meet strangers in the street, and love them.
LG1871	3835	Why, what have you thought of yourself?
LG1871	3836	Is it you then that thought yourself less?
LG1871	3837	Is it you that thought the President greater than you?
LG1871	3838	Or the rich better off than you? or the educated wiser than you?
LG1871	3839	Because you are greasy or pimpled, or that you were once drunk, or a thief,
LG1871	3840	Or diseas'd, or rheumatic, or a prostitute—or are so now;
LG1871	3841	Or from frivolity or impotence, or that you are no scholar, and never saw your name in print,
LG1871	3842	Do you give in that you are any less immortal?
LG1871	3843	Souls of men and women! it is not you I call unseen, unheard, untouchable and untouching;
LG1871	3844	It is not you I go argue pro and con about, and to settle whether you are alive or no;
LG1871	3845	I own publicly who you are, if nobody else owns.
LG1871	3846	Grown, half-grown, and babe, of this country and every country, in-doors and out-doors, one just as much as the other, I see,
LG1871	3847	And all else behind or through them.
LG1871	3848	The wife—and she is not one jot less than the husband;
LG1871	3849	The daughter—and she is just as good as the son;
LG1871	3850	The mother—and she is every bit as much as the father.
LG1871	3851	Offspring of ignorant and poor, boys apprenticed to trades,
LG1871	3852	Young fellows working on farms, and old fellows work-ingworking on farms,
LG1871	3853	Sailor-men, merchant-men, coasters, immigrants,
LG1871	3854	All these I see—but nigher and farther the same I see;
LG1871	3855	None shall escape me, and none shall wish to escape me.
LG1871	3856	I bring what you much need, yet always have,
LG1871	3857	Not money, amours, dress, eating, but as good;
LG1871	3858	I send no agent or medium, offer no representative of value, but offer the value itself.
LG1871	3859	There is something that comes home to one now and perpetually;
LG1871	3860	It is not what is printed, preach'd, discussed—it eludes discussion and print;
LG1871	3861	It is not to be put in a book—it is not in this book;
LG1871	3862	It is for you, whoever you are—it is no farther from you than your hearing and sight are from you;
LG1871	3863	It is hinted by nearest, commonest, readiest—it is ever provoked by them.
LG1871	3864	You may read in many languages, yet read nothing about it;
LG1871	3865	You may read the President's Message, and read noth-ingnothing about it there;
LG1871	3866	Nothing in the reports from the State department or Treasury department, or in the daily papers or the weekly papers,
LG1871	3867	Or in the census or revenue returns, prices current, or any accounts of stock.
LG1871	3868	The sun and stars that float in the open air;
LG1871	3869	The apple-shaped earth, and we upon it—surely the drift of them is something grand!
LG1871	3870	I do not know what it is, except that it is grand, and that it is happiness,
LG1871	3871	And that the enclosing purport of us here is not a speculation, or bon-mot, or reconnoissance,
LG1871	3872	And that it is not something which by luck may turn out well for us, and without luck must be a failure for us,
LG1871	3873	And not something which may yet be retracted in a certain contingency.
LG1871	3874	The light and shade, the curious sense of body and identity, the greed that with perfect complais-ancecomplaisance devours all things, the endless pride and out-stretching of man, unspeakable joys and sorrows,
LG1871	3875	The wonder every one sees in every one else he sees, and the wonders that fill each minute of time forever,
LG1871	3876	What have you reckon'd them for, camerado?
LG1871	3877	Have you reckon'd them for a trade, or farm-work? or for the profits of a store?
LG1871	3878	Or to achieve yourself a position? or to fill a gentle-man'sgentleman's leisure, or a lady's leisure?

LG1871	3880	Or men and women that they might be written of, and songs sung?

LG1871	3883	Or the stars to be put in constellations and named fancy names?

LG1871	3888	We thought our Union grand, and our Constitution grand;
LG1871	3889	I do not say they are not grand and good, for they are;

LG1871	3895	Leaves are not more shed from the trees, or trees from the earth, than they are shed out of you.

LG1871	3972	I WANDER all night in my vision,
LG1871	3973	Stepping with light feet, swiftly and noiselessly step-pingstepping and stopping,
LG1871	3974	Bending with open eyes over the shut eyes of sleepers,
LG1871	3975	Wandering and confused, lost to myself, ill-assorted, contradictory,
LG1871	3976	Pausing, gazing, bending, and stopping.
LG1871	3977	How solemn they look there, stretch'd and still!
LG1871	3978	How quiet they breathe, the little children in their cradles!
LG1871	3979	The wretched features of ennuyés, the white features of corpses, the livid faces of drunkards, the sick-graysick-gray faces of onanists,
LG1871	3980	The gash'd bodies on battle-fields, the insane in their strong-door'd rooms, the sacred idiots, the new-bornnew-born emerging from gates, and the dying emerg-ingemerging from gates,
LG1871	3981	The night pervades them and infolds them.
LG1871	3982	The married couple sleep calmly in their bed—he with his palm on the hip of the wife, and she with her palm on the hip of the husband,
LG1871	3983	The sisters sleep lovingly side by side in their bed,
LG1871	3984	The men sleep lovingly side by side in theirs,
LG1871	3985	And the mother sleeps, with her little child carefully wrapt.
LG1871	3986	The blind sleep, and the deaf and dumb sleep,
LG1871	3987	The prisoner sleeps well in the prison—the run-away son sleeps;
LG1871	3988	The murderer that is to be hung next day—how does he sleep?
LG1871	3989	And the murder'd person—how does he sleep?
LG1871	3990	The female that loves unrequited sleeps,
LG1871	3991	And the male that loves unrequited sleeps,
LG1871	3992	The head of the money-maker that plotted all day sleeps,
LG1871	3993	And the enraged and treacherous dispositions—all, all sleep.
LG1871	3994	I stand in the dark with drooping eyes by the worst-sufferingworst-suffering and the most restless,

LG1871	3997	Now I pierce the darkness—new beings appear,
LG1871	3998	The earth recedes from me into the night,
LG1871	3999	I saw that it was beautiful, and I see that what is not the earth is beautiful.
LG1871	4000	I go from bedside to bedside—I sleep close with the other sleepers, each in turn,
LG1871	4001	I dream in my dream all the dreams of the other dream-ersdreamers,
LG1871	4002	And I become the other dreamers.
LG1871	4003	I am a dance—Play up, there! the fit is whirling me fast!
LG1871	4004	I am the ever-laughing—it is new moon and twi-lighttwilight,
LG1871	4005	I see the hiding of douceurs—I see nimble ghosts whichever way I look,
LG1871	4006	Cache, and cache again, deep in the ground and sea, and where it is neither ground or sea.
LG1871	4007	Well do they do their jobs, those journeymen divine,
LG1871	4008	Only from me can they hide nothing, and would not if they could,
LG1871	4009	I reckon I am their boss, and they make me a pet besides,
LG1871	4010	And surround me and lead me, and run ahead when I walk,

LG1871	4012	Onward we move! a gay gang of blackguards! with mirth-shouting music, and wild-flapping pennants of joy!
LG1871	4013	I am the actor, the actress, the voter, the politician;
LG1871	4014	The emigrant and the exile, the criminal that stood in the box,
LG1871	4015	He who has been famous, and he who shall be famous after to-day,

LG1871	4018	My truant lover has come, and it is dark.
LG1871	4019	Double yourself and receive me, darkness!
LG1871	4020	Receive me and my lover too—he will not let me go without him.
LG1871	4021	I roll myself upon you, as upon a bed—I resign my-selfmyself to the dusk.
LG1871	4022	He whom I call answers me, and takes the place of my lover,

LG1871	4024	Darkness! you are gentler than my lover—his flesh was sweaty and panting,

LG1871	4026	My hands are spread forth, I pass them in all direc-tionsdirections,

LG1871	4028	Be careful, darkness! already, what was it touch'd me?
LG1871	4029	I thought my lover had gone, else darkness and he are one,
LG1871	4030	I hear the heart-beat—I follow, I fade away.
LG1871	4031	O hot-cheek'd and blushing! O foolish hectic!

LG1871	4547	THERE was a child went forth every day;

LG1871	4609	THINK of the Soul;
LG1871	4610	I swear to you that body of yours gives proportions to your Soul somehow to live in other spheres;
LG1871	4611	I do not know how, but I know it is so.

LG1871	4629	The creation is womanhood;

LG1871	4631	Have I not told how the universe has nothing better than the best womanhood?
LG1871	4632	YOU felons on trial in courts;

LG1871	4654	I WAS looking a long while for a clue to the history of the past for myself, and for these chants—and now I have found it;
LG1871	4655	It is not in those paged fables in the libraries, (them I neither accept nor reject;)

LG1871	4679	FIRST, O songs, for a prelude,

LG1871	4950	It is well—a lesson like that, always comes good;
LG1871	4951	I must copy the story, and send it eastward and west-wardwestward;
LG1871	4952	I must preserve that look, as it beam'd on you, rivers of Brooklyn.
LG1871	4953	See! as the annual round returns, the phantoms return;
LG1871	4954	It is the 27th of August, and the British have landed;
LG1871	4955	The battle begins, and goes against us—behold! through the smoke, Washington's face;
LG1871	4956	The brigade of Virginia and Maryland have march'd forth to intercept the enemy;
LG1871	4957	They are cut off—murderous artillery from the hills plays upon them;
LG1871	4958	Rank after rank falls, while over them silently droops the flag,
LG1871	4959	Baptized that day in many a young man's bloody wounds,
LG1871	4960	In death, defeat, and sisters', mothers' tears.
LG1871	4961	Ah, hills and slopes of Brooklyn! I perceive you are more valuable than your owners supposed;
LG1871	4962	Ah, river! henceforth you will be illumin'd to me at sunrise with something besides the sun.
LG1871	4963	Encampments new! in the midst of you stands an encampment very old;
LG1871	4964	Stands forever the camp of the dead brigade.
LG1871	4965	WITH its cloud of skirmishers in advance,
LG1871	4966	With now the sound of a single shot, snapping like a whip, and now an irregular volley,
LG1871	4967	The swarming ranks press on and on, the dense brigades press on;
LG1871	4968	Glittering dimly, toiling under the sun—the dust-cover'd men,
LG1871	4969	In columns rise and fall to the undulations of the ground,
LG1871	4970	With artillery interspers'd—the wheels rumble, the horses sweat,
LG1871	4971	As the army corps advances.
LG1871	4972	A LINE in long array, where they wind betwixt green islands;
LG1871	4973	They take a serpentine course—their arms flash in the sun—Hark to the musical clank;
LG1871	4974	Behold the silvery river—in it the splashing horses, loitering, stop to drink;
LG1871	4975	Behold the brown-faced men—each group, each person, a picture—the negligent rest on the saddles;

LG1871	4977	Scarlet, and blue, and snowy white,
LG1871	4978	The guidon flags flutter gaily in the wind.
LG1871	4979	I SEE before me now, a traveling army halting;
LG1871	4980	Below, a fertile valley spread, with barns, and the orchards of summer;
LG1871	4981	Behind, the terraced sides of a mountain, abrupt in places, rising high!
LG1871	4982	Broken, with rocks, with clinging cedars, with tall shapes, dingily seen;
LG1871	4983	The numerous camp-fires scatter'd near and far, some away up on the mountain;
LG1871	4984	The shadowy forms of men and horses, looming, large-sizedlarge-sized, flickering;

LG1871	4986	BY the bivouac's fitful flame,
LG1871	4987	A procession winding around me, solemn and sweet and slow;—but first I note,
LG1871	4988	The tents of the sleeping army, the fields' and woods' dim outline,
LG1871	4989	The darkness, lit by spots of kindled fire—the silence;
LG1871	4990	Like a phantom far or near an occasional figure moving;
LG1871	4991	The shrubs and trees, (as I left my eyes they seem to be stealthily watching me;)
LG1871	4992	While wind in procession thoughts, O tender and wondrous thoughts,
LG1871	4993	Of life and death—of home and the past and loved, and of those that are far away;
LG1871	4994	A solemn and slow procession there as I sit on the ground,
LG1871	4995	By the bivouac's fitful flame.
LG1871	4996	COME up from the fields, father, here's a letter from our Pete;
LG1871	4997	And come to the front door, mother—here's a letter from thy dear son.
LG1871	4998	Lo, 'tis autumn;
LG1871	4999	Lo, where the trees, deeper green, yellower and redder;
LG1871	5000	Cool and sweeten Ohio's villages, with leaves fluttering in the moderate wind;
LG1871	5001	Where apples ripe in the orchards hang, and grapes on the trellis'd vines;
LG1871	5002	(Smell you the smell of the grapes on the vines?
LG1871	5003	Smell you the buckwheat, where the bees were lately buzzing?)
LG1871	5004	Above all, lo, the sky, so calm, so transparent after the rain, and with wondrous clouds;

LG1871	5007	But now from the fields come, father—come at the daughter's call;

LG1871	5009	Fast as she can she hurries—something ominous— her steps trembling;

LG1871	5012	O this is not our son's writing, yet his name is sign'd;
LG1871	5013	O a strange hand writes for our dear son—O stricken mother's soul!

LG1871	5015	Sentences broken—gun-shot wound in the breast, cavalry skirmish, taken to hospital,
LG1871	5016	At present low, but will soon be better.
LG1871	5017	Ah, now the single figure to me,

LG1871	5020	By the jamb of a door leans.

LG1871	5023	See, dearest mother, the letter says Pete will soon be better.
LG1871	5024	Alas, poor boy, he will never be better, (nor may-be needs to be better, that brave and simple soul;)
LG1871	5025	While they stand at home at the door, he is dead already;
LG1871	5026	The only son is dead.
LG1871	5027	But the mother needs to be better;
LG1871	5028	She, with thin form, presently drest in black;
LG1871	5029	By day her meals untouch'd—then at night fitfully sleeping, often waking,

LG1871	5031	O that she might withdraw unnoticed—silent from life, escape and withdraw,
LG1871	5032	To follow, to seek, to be with her dear dead son.
LG1871	5033	VIGIL strange I kept on the field one night:
LG1871	5034	When you, my son and my comrade, dropt at my side that day,
LG1871	5035	One look I but gave, which your dear eyes return'd, with a look I shall never forget;
LG1871	5036	One touch of your hand to mine, O boy, reach'd up as you lay on the ground;
LG1871	5037	Then onward I sped in the battle, the even-contested battle;
LG1871	5038	Till late in the night reliev'd, to the place at last again I made my way;
LG1871	5039	Found you in death so cold, dear comrade—found your body, son of responding kisses, (never again on earth responding;)
LG1871	5040	Bared your face in the starlight—curious the scene— cool blew the moderate night-wind;
LG1871	5041	Long there and then in vigil I stood, dimly around me the battle-field spreading;
LG1871	5042	Vigil wondrous and vigil sweet, there in the fragrant silent night;
LG1871	5043	But not a tear fell, not even a long-drawn sigh—Long, long I gazed;
LG1871	5044	Then on the earth partially reclining, sat by your side, leaning my chin in my hands;
LG1871	5045	Passing sweet hours, immortal and mystic hours with you, dearest comrade—Not a tear, not a word;
LG1871	5046	Vigil of silence, love and death—vigil for you, my son and my soldier,
LG1871	5047	As onward silently stars aloft, eastward new ones up-wardupward stole;
LG1871	5048	Vigil final for you, brave boy, (I could not save you, swift was your death,
LG1871	5049	I faithfully loved you and cared for you living—I think we shall surely meet again;)
LG1871	5050	Till at latest lingering of the night, indeed just as the dawn appear'd,
LG1871	5051	My comrade I wrapt in his blanket, envelop'd well his form,
LG1871	5052	Folded the blanket well, tucking it carefully over head, and carefully under feet;
LG1871	5053	And there and then, and bathed by the rising sun, my son in his grave, in his rude-dug grave I de-positeddeposited;
LG1871	5054	Ending my vigil strange with that—vigil of night and battle-field dim;
LG1871	5055	Vigil for boy of responding kisses, (never again on earth responding;)

LG1871	5057	I rose from the chill ground, and folded my soldier well in his blanket,
LG1871	5058	And buried him where he fell.
LG1871	5059	A MARCH in the ranks hard-prest, and the road unknown;
LG1871	5060	A route through a heavy wood, with muffled steps in the darkness;
LG1871	5061	Our army foil'd with loss severe, and the sullen remnant retreating;
LG1871	5062	Till after midnight glimmer upon us, the lights of a dim-lighted building;
LG1871	5063	We come to an open space in the woods, and halt by the dim-lighted building;
LG1871	5064	'Tis a large old church at the crossing roads—'tis now an impromptu hospital;
LG1871	5065	—Entering but for a minute, I see a sight beyond all the pictures and poems ever made;
LG1871	5066	Shadows of deepest, deepest black, just lit by moving candles and lamps,

LG1871	5073	Surgeons operating, attendants holding lights, the smell of ether, the odor of blood;

LG1871	5075	Some on the bare ground, some on planks or stretchers, some in the death-spasm sweating;

LG1871	5079	Then hear outside the orders given, Fall in, my men, Fall in;
LG1871	5080	But first I bend to the dying lad—his eyes open—a half-smile gives he me;
LG1871	5081	Then the eyes close, calmly close, and I speed forth to the darkness,
LG1871	5082	Resuming, marching, ever in darkness marching, on in the ranks,

LG1871	5084	A SIGHT in camp in the day-break grey and dim,

LG1871	5099	NOT the pilot has charged himself to bring his ship into port, though beaten back, and many times baffled;

LG1871	5184	LONG, too long, O land,

LG1871	5229	THE last sunbeam
LG1871	5230	Lightly falls from the finish'd Sabbath,
LG1871	5231	On the pavement here—and there beyond, it is looking, Down a new-made double grave.
LG1871	5232	Lo! the moon ascending!
LG1871	5233	Up from the east, the silvery round moon;
LG1871	5234	Beautiful over the house-tops, ghastly, phantom moon; Immense and silent moon.
LG1871	5235	I see a sad procession,

LG1871	5238	I hear the great drums pounding,

LG1871	5241	For the son is brought with the father;
LG1871	5242	In the foremost ranks of the fierce assault they fell;
LG1871	5243	Two veterans, son and father, dropt together, And the double grave awaits them.

LG1871	5251	O moon immense, with your silvery face you soothe me!

LG1871	5256	OVER the carnage rose prophetic a voice,
LG1871	5257	Be not dishearten'd—Affection shall solve the problems of Freedom yet;
LG1871	5258	Those who love each other shall become invincible— they shall yet make Columbia victorious.
LG1871	5259	Sons of the Mother of All! you shall yet be victo-riousvictorious!

LG1871	5263	One from Massachusetts shall be a Missourian's com-radecomrade;
LG1871	5264	From Maine and from hot Carolina, and another, an Oregonese, shall be friends triune,
LG1871	5265	More precious to each other than all the riches of the earth.
LG1871	5266	To Michigan, Florida, perfumes shall tenderly come;
LG1871	5267	Not the perfumes of flowers, but sweeter, and wafted beyond death.
LG1871	5268	It shall be customary in the houses and streets to see manly affection;
LG1871	5269	The most dauntless and rude shall touch face to face lightly;
LG1871	5270	The dependence of Liberty shall be lovers,
LG1871	5271	The continuance of Equality shall be comrades.
LG1871	5272	These shall tie you and band you stronger than hoops of iron;
LG1871	5273	I, extatic, O partners! O lands! with the love of lovers tie you.
LG1871	5274	(Were you looking to be held together by the lawyers?
LG1871	5275	Or by an agreement on a paper? or by arms?
LG1871	5276	—Nay—nor the world, nor any living thing, will so cohere.)
LG1871	5277	WHILE my wife at my side lies slumbering, and the wars are over long,
LG1871	5278	And my head on the pillow rests at home, and the va-cantvacant midnight passes,
LG1871	5279	And through the stillness, through the dark, I hear, just hear, the breath of my infant,
LG1871	5280	There in the room, as I wake from sleep, this vision presses upon me:
LG1871	5281	The engagement opens there and then, in fantasy unreal;
LG1871	5282	The skirmishers begin—they crawl cautiously ahead— I hear the irregular snap! snap!
LG1871	5283	I hear the sounds of the different missiles—the short t-h-t! t-h-t! of the rifle balls;
LG1871	5284	I see the shells exploding, leaving small white clouds— I hear the great shells shieking as they pass;
LG1871	5285	The grape, like the hum and whirr of wind through the trees, (quick, tumultuous, now the contest rages!)
LG1871	5286	All the scenes at the batteries themselves rise in detail before me again;
LG1871	5287	The crashing and smoking—the pride of the men in their pieces;
LG1871	5288	The chief gunner ranges and sights his piece, and se-lectsselects a fuse of the right time;
LG1871	5289	After firing, I see him lean aside, and look eagerly off to note the effect;
LG1871	5290	—Elsewhere I hear the cry of a regiment charging— (the young colonel leads himself this time, with brandish'd sword;)
LG1871	5291	I see the gaps cut by the enemy's volleys, (quickly fill'd up, no delay;)
LG1871	5292	I breathe the suffocating smoke—then the flat clouds hover low, concealing all;
LG1871	5293	Now a strange lull comes for a few seconds, not a shot fired on either side;
LG1871	5294	Then resumed, the chaos louder than ever, with eager calls, and orders of officers;
LG1871	5295	While from some distant part of the field the wind wafts to my ears a shout of applause, (some special success;)
LG1871	5296	And ever the sound of the cannon, far or near, (rousing, even in dreams, a devilish exultation, and all the old mad joy, in the depths of my soul;)
LG1871	5297	And ever the hastening of infantry shifting positions— batteries, cavalry, moving hither and thither;
LG1871	5298	(The falling, dying, I heed not—the wounded, dripping and red, I heed not—some to the rear are hob-blinghobbling;)
LG1871	5299	Grime, heat, rush—aid-de-camps galloping by, or on a full run;
LG1871	5300	With the patter of small arms, the warning s-s-t of the rifles, (these in my vision I hear or see,)
LG1871	5301	And bombs bursting in air, and at night the vari-color'dvari-color'd rockets.
LG1871	5302	I SAW old General at bay;
LG1871	5303	(Old as he was, his grey eyes yet shone out in battle like stars;)
LG1871	5304	His small force was now completely hemm'd in, in his works;
LG1871	5305	He call'd for volunteers to run the enemy's lines—a desperate emergency;
LG1871	5306	I saw a hundred and more step forth from the ranks— but two or three were selected;
LG1871	5307	I saw them receive their orders aside—they listen'd with care—the adjutant was very grave;
LG1871	5308	I saw them depart with cheerfulness, freely risking their lives.
LG1871	5309	O TAN-FACED prairie-boy!
LG1871	5310	Before you came to camp, came many a welcome gift;
LG1871	5311	Praises and presents came, and nourishing food—till at last, among the recruits,
LG1871	5312	You came, taciturn, with nothing to give—we but look'd on each other,
LG1871	5313	When lo! more than all the gifts of the world, you gave me.
LG1871	5314	LOOK down, fair moon, and bathe this scene;
LG1871	5315	Pour softly down night's nimbus floods, on faces ghast-lyghastly, swollen, purple;
LG1871	5316	On the dead, on their backs, with their arms toss'd wide,
LG1871	5317	Pour down your unstinted nimbus, sacred moon.
LG1871	5318	WORD over all, beautiful as the sky!
LG1871	5319	Beautiful that war, and all its deeds of carnage, must in time be utterly lost;
LG1871	5320	That the hands of the sisters Death and Night, inces-santlyincessantly softly wash again, and ever again, this soil'd world:
LG1871	5321	…For my enemy is dead—a man divine as myself is dead;
LG1871	5322	I look where he lies, white-faced and still, in the coffin —I draw near;
LG1871	5323	I bend down, and touch lightly with my lips the white face in the coffin.
LG1871	5324	SPIRIT whose work is done! spirit of dreadful hours!
LG1871	5325	Ere, departing, fade from my eyes your forests of bayo-netsbayonets;
LG1871	5326	Spirit of gloomiest fears and doubts, (yet onward ever unfaltering pressing;)
LG1871	5327	Spirit of many a solemn day, and many a savage scene! Electric spirit!
LG1871	5328	That with muttering voice, through the war now closed, like a tireless phantom flitted,
LG1871	5329	Rousing the land with breath of flame, while you beat and beat the drum;
LG1871	5330	—Now, as the sound of the drum, hollow and harsh to the last, reverberates round me;
LG1871	5331	As your ranks, your immortal ranks, return, return from the battles;
LG1871	5332	While the muskets of the young men yet lean over their shoulders;
LG1871	5333	While I look on the bayonets bristling over their shoul-dersshoulders;
LG1871	5334	While those slanted bayonets, whole forests of them, appearing in the distance, approach and pass on, returning homeward,
LG1871	5335	Moving with steady motion, swaying to and fro, to the right and left,
LG1871	5336	Evenly, lightly rising and falling, as the steps keep time:
LG1871	5337	—Spirit of hours I knew, all hectic red one day, but pale as death next day;
LG1871	5338	Touch my mouth, ere you depart—press my lips close!
LG1871	5339	Leave me your pulses of rage! bequeath them to me! fill me with currents convulsive!
LG1871	5340	Let them scorch and blister out of my chants, when you are gone;
LG1871	5341	Let them identify you to the future, in these songs.
LG1871	5342	HOW solemn, as one by one,
LG1871	5343	As the ranks returning, all worn and sweaty—as the men file by where I stand;
LG1871	5344	As the faces, the masks appear—as I glance at the faces, studying the masks;

LG1871	5346	How solemn the thought of my whispering soul, to each in the ranks, and to you;
LG1871	5347	I see behind each mask, that wonder, a kindred soul;
LG1871	5348	O the bullet could never kill what you really are, dear friend,
LG1871	5349	Nor the bayonet stab what you really are:
LG1871	5350	…The soul! yourself I see, great as any, good as the best,
LG1871	5351	Waiting, secure and content, which the bullet could never kill,
LG1871	5352	Nor the bayonet stab, O friend!
LG1871	5353	NOT youth pertains to me,
LG1871	5354	Nor delicatesse—I cannot beguile the time with talk;
LG1871	5355	Awkward in the parlor, neither a dancer nor elegant;
LG1871	5356	In the learn'd coterie sitting constrain'd and still—for learning inures not to me;
LG1871	5357	Beauty, knowledge, inure not to me—yet there are two or three things inure to me;
LG1871	5358	I have nourish'd the wounded, and sooth'd many a dying soldier.
LG1871	5359	And at intervals, waiting, or in the midst of camp,
LG1871	5360	Composed these songs.
LG1871	5361	TO the leaven'd soil they trod, calling, I sing, for the last;
LG1871	5362	(Not cities, nor man alone, nor war, nor the dead,
LG1871	5363	But forth from my tent emerging for good—loosing, untying the tent-ropes;)
LG1871	5364	In the freshness, the forenoon air, in the far-stretching circuits and vistas, again to peace restored,
LG1871	5365	To the fiery fields emanative, and the endless vistas beyond—to the south and the north;
LG1871	5366	To the leaven'd soil of the general western world, to attest my songs,
LG1871	5367	(To the average earth, the wordless earth, witness of war and peace,)
LG1871	5368	To the Alleghanian hills, and the tireless Mississippi,
LG1871	5369	To the rocks I, calling, sing, and all the trees in the woods,
LG1871	5370	To the plain of the poems of heroes, to the prairie spreading wide,
LG1871	5371	To the far-off sea, and the unseen winds, and the sane impalpable air;
LG1871	5372	…And responding, they answer all, (but not in words,)
LG1871	5373	The average earth, the witness of war and peace, acknowledges mutely;
LG1871	5374	The prairie draws me close, as the father, to bosom broad, the son;
LG1871	5375	The Northern ice and rain, that began me, nourish me to the end;
LG1871	5376	But the hot sun of the South is to ripen my songs.
LG1871	5377	SAUNTERING the pavement, or riding the country by-roadby-road—lo! such faces!
