LG1871	5392	Do you suppose I could be content with all, if I thought them their own finale?
LG1871	5393	This now is too lamentable a face for a man;
LG1871	5394	Some abject louse, asking leave to be—cringing for it;
LG1871	5395	Some milk-nosed maggot, blessing what lets it wrig to its hole.
LG1871	5396	This face is a dog's snout, sniffing for garbage;
LG1871	5397	Snakes nest in that mouth—I hear the sibilant threat.
LG1871	5398	This face is a haze more chill than the arctic sea;
LG1871	5399	Its sleepy and wobbling icebergs crunch as they go.
LG1871	5400	This is a face of bitter herbs—this an emetic—they need no label;
LG1871	5401	And more of the drug-shelf, laudanum, caoutchouc, or hog's-lard.
LG1871	5402	This face is an epilepsy, its wordless tongue gives out the unearthly cry,
LG1871	5403	Its veins down the neck distend, its eyes roll till they show nothing but their whites,
LG1871	5404	Its teeth grit, the palms of the hands are cut by the turn'd-in nails,
LG1871	5405	The man falls struggling and foaming to the ground while he speculates well.
LG1871	5406	This face is bitten by vermin and worms,
LG1871	5407	And this is some murderer's knife, with a half-pull'd scabbard.
LG1871	5408	This face owes to the sexton his dismalest fee;
LG1871	5409	An unceasing death-bell tolls there.
LG1871	5410	Those then are really men—the bosses and tufts of the great round globe!
LG1871	5411	Features of my equals, would you trick me with your creas'd and cadaverous march?
LG1871	5412	Well, you cannot trick me.
LG1871	5413	I see your rounded, never-erased flow;
LG1871	5414	I see neath the rims of your haggard and mean dis-guisesdisguises.
LG1871	5415	Splay and twist as you like—poke with the tangling fores of fishes or rats;
LG1871	5416	You'll be unmuzzled, you certainly will.
LG1871	5417	I saw the face of the most smear'd and slobbering idiot they had at the asylum;
LG1871	5418	And I knew for my consolation what they knew not;
LG1871	5419	I knew of the agents that emptied and broke my brother,
LG1871	5420	The same wait to clear the rubbish from the fallen ten-ementtenement;
LG1871	5421	And I shall look again in a score or two of ages,
LG1871	5422	And I shall meet the real landlord, perfect and un-harm'dunharm'd, every inch as good as myself.
LG1871	5423	The Lord advances, and yet advances;
LG1871	5424	Always the shadow in front—always the reach'd hand, bringing up the laggards.

LG1871	5426	I see the high pioneer-caps—I see the staves of runners clearing the way,

LG1871	5428	This face is a life-boat;
LG1871	5429	This is the face commanding and bearded, it asks no odds of the rest;
LG1871	5430	This face is flavor'd fruit, ready for eating;
LG1871	5431	This face of a healthy honest boy is the programme of all good.
LG1871	5432	These faces bear testimony, slumbering or awake;
LG1871	5433	They show their descent from the Master himself.
LG1871	5434	Off the word I have spoken I except not one—red, white, black, are all deific;
LG1871	5435	In each house is the ovum—it comes forth after a thou-sandthousand years.
LG1871	5436	Spots or cracks at the windows do not disturb me;
LG1871	5437	Tall and sufficient stand behind, and make signs to me;
LG1871	5438	I read the promise, and patiently wait.
LG1871	5439	This is a full-grown lily's face,
LG1871	5440	She speaks to the limber-hipp'd man near the garden pickets,
LG1871	5441	Come here, she blushingly cries—Come nigh to me, lim-ber-hipp'dlimber-hipp'd man,

LG1871	5443	Fill me with albescent honey, bend down to me,
LG1871	5444	Rub to me with your chafing beard, rub to my breast and shoulders.
LG1871	5445	The old face of the mother of many children!
LG1871	5446	Whist! I am fully content.
LG1871	5447	Lull'd and late is the smoke of the First-day morning,
LG1871	5448	It hangs low over the rows of trees by the fences,
LG1871	5449	It hangs thin by the sassafras, the wild-cherry, and the cat-brier under them.
LG1871	5450	I saw the rich ladies in full dress at the soiree,
LG1871	5451	I heard what the singers were singing so long.
LG1871	5452	Heard who sprang in crimson youth from the white froth and the water-blue.
LG1871	5453	Behold a woman!
LG1871	5454	She looks out from her quaker cap—her face is clearer and more beautiful than the sky.
LG1871	5455	She sits in an arm-chair, under the shaded porch of the farm-house,
LG1871	5456	The sun just shines on her old white head.
LG1871	5457	Her ample gown is of cream-hued linen,
LG1871	5458	Her grandsons raised the flax, and her granddaugh-tersgranddaughters spun it with the distaff and the wheel.
LG1871	5459	The melodious character of the earth,
LG1871	5460	The finish beyond which philosophy cannot go, and does not wish to go,
LG1871	5461	The justified mother of men.
LG1871	5462	MANHATTAN'S streets I saunter'd, pondering,
LG1871	5463	On time, space, reality—on such as these, and abreast with them, prudence.
LG1871	5464	After all, the last explanation remains to be made about prudence;
LG1871	5465	Little and large alike drop quietly aside from the pru-denceprudence that suits immortality.
LG1871	5466	The Soul is of itself;
LG1871	5467	All verges to it—all has reference to what ensues;
LG1871	5468	All that a person does, says, thinks, is of consequence;
LG1871	5469	Not a move can a man or woman make, that affects him or her in a day, month, any part of the direct life-time, or the hour of death, but the same affects him or her onward afterward through the indirect life-time.
LG1871	5470	The indirect is just as much as the direct,
LG1871	5471	The spirit receives from the body just as much as it gives to the body, if not more.
LG1871	5472	Not one word or deed—not venereal sore, discolor-ationdiscoloration, privacy of the onanist, putridity of glut-tonsgluttons or rum-drinkers, peculation, cunning, betrayal, murder, seduction, prostitution, but has results beyond death, as really as before death.
LG1871	5473	Charity and personal force are the only investments worth anything.
LG1871	5474	No specification is necessary—all that a male or fe-malefemale does, that is vigorous, benevolent, clean, is so much profit to him or her, in the unshake-ableunshakable order of the universe, and through the whole scope of it, forever.
LG1871	5475	Who has been wise, receives interest,
LG1871	5476	Savage, felon, President, judge, farmer, sailor, me-chanicmechanic, literat, young, old, it is the same,
LG1871	5477	The interest will come round—all will come round.
LG1871	5478	Singly, wholly, to affect now, affected their time, will forever affect, all of the past, and all of the present, and all of the future,
LG1871	5479	All the brave actions of war and peace,
LG1871	5480	All help given to relatives, strangers, the poor, old, sorrowful, young children, widows, the sick, and to shunn'd persons,
LG1871	5481	All furtherance of fugitives, and of the escape of slaves,
LG1871	5482	All self-denial that stood steady and aloof on wrecks, and saw others fill the seats of the boats,
LG1871	5483	All offering of substance or life for the good old cause, or for a friend's sake, or opinion's sake,
LG1871	5484	All pains of enthusiasts, scoff'd at by their neighbors,
LG1871	5485	All the limitless sweet love and precious suffering of mothers,
LG1871	5486	All honest men baffled in strifes recorded or unre-cordedunrecorded,
LG1871	5487	All the grandeur and good of ancient nations whose fragments we inherit,
LG1871	5488	All the good of the dozens of ancient nations un-knownunknown to us by name, date, location,
LG1871	5489	All that was ever manfully begun, whether it suc-ceededsucceeded or no,
LG1871	5490	All suggestions of the divine mind of man, or the divinity of his mouth, or the shaping of his great hands;
LG1871	5491	All that is well thought or said this day on any part of the globe—or on any of the wandering stars, or on any of the fix'd stars, by those there as we are here;
LG1871	5492	All that is henceforth to be thought or done by you, whoever you are, or by any one;
LG1871	5493	These inure, have inured, shall inure, to the identities from which they sprang, or shall spring.
LG1871	5494	Did you guess anything lived only its moment?
LG1871	5495	The world does not so exist—no parts palpable or im-palpableimpalpable so exist;
LG1871	5496	No consummation exists without being from some long previous consummation—and that from some other,
LG1871	5497	Without the farthest conceivable one coming a bit nearer the beginning than any.
LG1871	5498	Whatever satisfies Souls is true;
LG1871	5499	Prudence entirely satisfies the craving and glut of Souls;
LG1871	5500	Itself only finally satisfies the Soul;
LG1871	5501	The Soul has that measureless pride which revolts from every lesson but its own.
LG1871	5502	Now I give you an inkling;
LG1871	5503	Now I breathe the word of the prudence that walks abreast with time, space, reality,
LG1871	5504	That answers the pride which refuses every lesson but its own.
LG1871	5505	What is prudence, is indivisible,
LG1871	5506	Declines to separate one part of life from every part,
LG1871	5507	Divides not the righteous from the unrighteous, or the living from the dead,
LG1871	5508	Matches every thought or act by its correlative,
LG1871	5509	Knows no possible forgiveness, or deputed atonement,
LG1871	5510	Knows that the young man who composedly peril'd his life and lost it, has done exceedingly well for himself, without doubt,
LG1871	5511	That he who never peril'd his life, but retains it to old age in riches and ease, has probably achiev'd nothing for himself worth mentioning;
LG1871	5512	Knows that only that person has really learn'd, who has learn'd to prefer results,
LG1871	5513	Who favors Body and Soul the same,
LG1871	5514	Who perceives the indirect assuredly following the direct,
LG1871	5515	Who in his spirit in any emergency whatever neither hurries or avoids death.
LG1871	5516	O ME, man of slack faith so long!
LG1871	5517	Standing aloof—denying portions so long;
LG1871	5518	Only aware to-day of compact, all-diffused truth;
LG1871	5519	Discovering to-day there is no lie, or form of lie, and can be none, but grows as inevitably upon itself as the truth does upon itself,
LG1871	5520	Or as any law of the earth, or any natural production of the earth does.
LG1871	5521	(This is curious, and may not be realized immediately —But it must be realized;
LG1871	5522	I feel in myself that I represent falsehoods equally with the rest,
LG1871	5523	And that the universe does.)
LG1871	5524	Where has fail'd a perfect return, indifferent of lies or the truth?
LG1871	5525	Is it upon the ground, or in water or fire? or in the spirit of man? or in the meat and blood?
LG1871	5526	Meditating among liars, and retreating sternly into myself, I see that there are really no liars or lies after all,
LG1871	5527	And that nothing fails its perfect return—And that what are called lies are perfect returns,
LG1871	5528	And that each thing exactly represents itself, and what has preceded it,
LG1871	5529	And that the truth includes all, and is compact, just as much as space is compact,
LG1871	5530	And that there is no flaw or vacuum in the amount of the truth—but that all is truth without excep-tionexception;
LG1871	5531	And henceforth I will go celebrate anything I see or am,
LG1871	5532	And sing and laugh, and deny nothing.
LG1871	5533	NOW I make a leaf of Voices—for I have found nothing mightier than they are,
LG1871	5534	And I have found that no word spoken, but is beautiful, in its place.
LG1871	5535	O what is it in me that makes me tremble so at voices?
LG1871	5536	Surely, whoever speaks to me in the right voice, him or her I shall follow,
LG1871	5537	As the water follows the moon, silently, with fluid steps, anywhere around the globe.
LG1871	5538	All waits for the right voices;
LG1871	5539	Where is the practis'd and perfect organ? Where is the develop'd Soul?
LG1871	5540	For I see every word utter'd thence, has deeper, sweeter, new sounds, impossible on less terms.
LG1871	5541	I see brains and lips closed—tympans and temples unstruck,
LG1871	5542	Until that comes which has the quality to strike and to unclose,
LG1871	5543	Until that comes which has the quality to bring forth what lies slumbering, forever ready, in all words.
LG1871	5544	As I sat alone, by blue Ontario's shore,
LG1871	5545	As I mused of these mighty days, and of peace return'd, and the dead that return no more,
LG1871	5546	A Phantom, gigantic, superb, with stern visage, accosted me;
LG1871	5547	Chant me the poem, it said, that comes from the soul of America—chant me the carol of victory;
LG1871	5548	And strike up the marches of Libertad—marches more powerful yet;
LG1871	5549	And sing me before you go, the song of the throes of Democracy.
LG1871	5550	(Democracy—the destin'd conqueror—yet treacherous lip-smiles everywhere,
LG1871	5551	And Death and infidelity at every step.)
LG1871	5552	A Nation announcing itself,
LG1871	5553	I myself make the only growth by which I can be ap-preciatedappreciated,
LG1871	5554	I reject none, accept all, then reproduce all in my own forms.
LG1871	5555	A breed whose proof is in time and deeds;
LG1871	5556	What we are, we are—nativity is answer enough to objections;
LG1871	5557	We wield ourselves as a weapon is wielded,
LG1871	5558	We are powerful and tremendous in ourselves,
LG1871	5559	We are executive in ourselves—We are sufficient in the variety of ourselves,
LG1871	5560	We are the most beautiful to ourselves, and in ourselves;
LG1871	5561	We stand self-pois'd in the middle, branching thence over the world;
LG1871	5562	From Missouri, Nebraska, or Kansas, laughing attacks to scorn.
LG1871	5563	Nothing is sinful to us outside of ourselves,
LG1871	5564	Whatever appears, whatever does not appear, we are beautiful or sinful in ourselves only.
LG1871	5565	(O mother! O sisters dear!
LG1871	5566	If we are lost, no victor else has destroy'd us;
LG1871	5567	It is by ourselves we go down to eternal night.)
LG1871	5568	Have you thought there could be but a single Supreme?
LG1871	5569	There can be any number of Supremes—One does not countervail another, any more than one eyesight countervails another, or one life countervails another.
LG1871	5570	All is eligible to all,
LG1871	5571	All is for individuals—All is for you,
LG1871	5572	No condition is prohibited—not God's, or any.
LG1871	5573	All comes by the body—only health puts you rapport with the universe.
LG1871	5574	Produce great persons, the rest follows.
LG1871	5575	America isolated I sing;
LG1871	5576	I say that works made here in the spirit of other lands, are so much poison in The States.
LG1871	5577	(How dare such insects as we see assume to write poems for America?
LG1871	5578	For our victorious armies, and the offspring following the armies?)
LG1871	5579	Piety and conformity to them that like!
LG1871	5580	Peace, obesity, allegiance, to them that like!
LG1871	5581	I am he who tauntingly compels men, women, nations,
LG1871	5582	Crying, Leap from your seats, and contend for your lives!
LG1871	5583	I am he who walks the States with a barb'd tongue, questioning every one I meet;
LG1871	5584	Who are you, that wanted only to be told what you knew before?
LG1871	5585	Who are you, that wanted only a book to join you in your nonsense?
LG1871	5586	(With pangs and cries, as thine own, O bearer of many children!
LG1871	5587	These clamors wild, to a race of pride I give.)
LG1871	5588	O lands! would you be freer than all that has ever been before!
LG1871	5589	If you would be freer than all that has been before, come listen to me.
LG1871	5590	Fear grace—Fear elegance, civilization, delicatesse,
LG1871	5591	Fear the mellow sweet, the sucking of honey-juice;
LG1871	5592	Beware the advancing mortal ripening of nature,
LG1871	5593	Beware what precedes the decay of the ruggedness of states and men.
LG1871	5594	Ages, precedents, have long been accumulating undi-rectedundirected materials,
LG1871	5595	America brings builders, and brings its own styles.
LG1871	5596	The immortal poets of Asia and Europe have done their work, and pass'd to other spheres,
LG1871	5597	A work remains, the work of surpassing all they have done.
LG1871	5598	America, curious toward foreign characters, stands by its own at all hazards,
LG1871	5599	Stands removed, spacious, composite, sound—initiates the true use of precedents,
LG1871	5600	Does not repel them, or the past, or what they have produced under their forms,
LG1871	5601	Takes the lesson with calmness, perceives the corpse slowly borne from the house,
LG1871	5602	Perceives that it waits a little while in the door—that it was fittest for its days,
LG1871	5603	That its life has descended to the stalwart and well-shapedwell-shaped heir who approaches,
LG1871	5604	And that he shall be fittest for his days.
LG1871	5605	Any period, one nation must lead,
LG1871	5606	One land must be the promise and reliance of the future.
LG1871	5607	These States are the amplest poem,
LG1871	5608	Here is not merely a nation, but a teeming nation of nations,
LG1871	5609	Here the doings of men correspond with the broadcast doings of the day and night,
LG1871	5610	Here is what moves in magnificent masses, careless of particulars,
LG1871	5611	Here are the roughs, beards, friendliness, combative-nesscombativeness, the Soul loves,
LG1871	5612	Here the flowing trains—here the crowds, equality, diversity, the Soul loves.
LG1871	5613	Land of lands, and bards to corroborate!
LG1871	5614	Of them, standing among them, one lifts to the light his west-bred face,
LG1871	5615	To him the hereditary countenance bequeath'd, both mother's and father's,
LG1871	5616	His first parts substances, earth, water, animals, trees,
LG1871	5617	Built of the common stock, having room for far and near,
LG1871	5618	Used to dispense with other lands, incarnating this land,
LG1871	5619	Attracting it Body and Soul to himself, hanging on its neck with incomparable love,
LG1871	5620	Plunging his seminal muscle into its merits and de-meritsdemerits,
LG1871	5621	Making its cities, beginnings, events, diversities, wars, vocal in him,
LG1871	5622	Making its rivers, lakes, bays, embouchure in him,
LG1871	5623	Mississippi with yearly freshets and changing chutes— Columbia, Niagara, Hudson, spending them-selvesthemselves lovingly in him,
LG1871	5624	If the Atlantic coast stretch, or the Pacific coast stretch, he stretching with them north or south,
LG1871	5625	Spanning between them, east and west, and touching whatever is between them,
LG1871	5626	Growths growing from him to offset the growth of pine, cedar, hemlock, live-oak, locust, chestnut, hickory, cotton-wood, orange, magnolia,
LG1871	5627	Tangles as tangled in him as any cane-brake or swamp,
LG1871	5628	He likening sides and peaks of mountains, forests coated with northern transparent ice,
LG1871	5629	Off him pasturage sweet and natural as savanna, up-landupland, prairie,
LG1871	5630	Through him flights, whirls, screams, answering those of the fish-hawk, mocking-bird, night-heron, and eagle;
LG1871	5631	His spirit surrounding his country's spirit, unclosed to good and evil,
LG1871	5632	Surrounding the essences of real things, old times and present times,
LG1871	5633	Surrounding just found shores, islands, tribes of red aborigines,
LG1871	5634	Weather-beaten vessels, landings, settlements, embryo stature and muscle,
LG1871	5635	The haughty defiance of the Year 1—war, peace, the formation of the Constitution,
LG1871	5636	The separate States, the simple, elastic scheme, the im-migrantsimmigrants,
LG1871	5637	The Union, always swarming with blatherers, and always sure and impregnable,
LG1871	5638	The unsurvey'd interior, log-houses, clearings, wild animals, hunters, trappers;
LG1871	5639	Surrounding the multiform agriculture, mines, tem-peraturetemperature, the gestation of new States,
LG1871	5640	Congress convening every Twelfth-month, the mem-bersmembers duly coming up from the uttermost parts;
LG1871	5641	Surrounding the noble character of mechanics and farmers, especially the young men,
LG1871	5642	Responding their manners, speech, dress, friendships— the gait they have of persons who never knew how it felt to stand in the presence of superiors,
LG1871	5643	The freshness and candor of their physiognomy, the copiousness and decision of their phrenology,
LG1871	5644	The picturesque looseness of their carriage, their fierce-nessfierceness when wrong'd,
LG1871	5645	The fluency of their speech, their delight in music, their curiosity, good temper, and open-handedness— the whole composite make,
LG1871	5646	The prevailing ardor and enterprise, the large amative-nessamativeness,
LG1871	5647	The perfect equality of the female with the male, the fluid movement of the population,
LG1871	5648	The superior marine, free commerce, fisheries, whaling, gold-digging,
LG1871	5649	Wharf-hemm'd cities, railroad and steamboat lines, in-tersectingintersecting all points,
LG1871	5650	Factories, mercantile life, labor-saving machinery, the north-east, north-west, south-west,
LG1871	5651	Manhattan firemen, the Yankee swap, southern planta-tionplantation life,
LG1871	5652	Slavery—the murderous, treacherous conspiracy to raise it upon the ruins of all the rest;
LG1871	5653	On and on to the grapple with it—Assassin! then your life or ours be the stake—and respite no more.
LG1871	5654	(Lo! high toward heaven, this day,
LG1871	5655	Libertad! from the conqueress' field return'd,
LG1871	5656	I mark the new aureola around your head;
LG1871	5657	No more of soft astral, but dazzling and fierce,
LG1871	5658	With war's flames, and the lambent lightnings playing,
LG1871	5659	And your port immovable where you stand;
LG1871	5660	With still the inextinguishable glance, and the clench'd and lifted fist,
LG1871	5661	And your foot on the neck of the menacing one, the scorner, utterly crush'd beneath you;
LG1871	5662	The menacing, arrogant one, that strode and advanced with his senseless scorn, bearing the murderous knife;
LG1871	5663	—Lo! the wide swelling one, the braggart, that would yesterday do so much!
LG1871	5664	To-day a carrion dead and damn'd, the despised of all the earth!
LG1871	5665	An offal rank, to the dunghill maggots spurn'd.)
LG1871	5666	Others take finish, but the Republic is ever construc-tiveconstructive, and ever keeps vista;
LG1871	5667	Others adorn the past—but you, O days of the present, I adorn you!
LG1871	5668	O days of the future, I believe in you! I isolate myself for your sake;
LG1871	5669	O America, because you build for mankind, I build for you!
LG1871	5670	O well-beloved stone-cutters! I lead them who plan with decision and science,
LG1871	5671	I lead the present with friendly hand toward the fu-turefuture.
LG1871	5672	Bravas to all impulses sending sane children to the next age!
LG1871	5673	But damn that which spends itself, with no thought of the stain, pains, dismay, feebleness, it is be-queathingbequeathing.
LG1871	5674	I listened to the Phantom on Ontario's shore,
LG1871	5675	I heard the voice arising, demanding bards;
LG1871	5676	By them all native and grand—by them alone can The States be fused into the compact organism of a Nation.
LG1871	5677	To hold men together by paper and seal, or by com-pulsioncompulsion, is no account;
LG1871	5678	That only holds men together which aggregates all in a living principle, as the hold of the limbs of the body, or the fibres of plants.
LG1871	5679	Of all races and eras, These States, with veins full of poetical stuff, most need poets, and are to have the greatest, and use them the greatest;
LG1871	5680	Their Presidents shall not be their common referee so much as their poets shall.
LG1871	5681	(Soul of love, and tongue of fire!
LG1871	5682	Eye to pierce the deepest deeps, and sweep the world!
LG1871	5683	—Ah, mother! prolific and full in all besides—yet how long barren, barren?)
LG1871	5684	Of These States, the poet is the equable man,
LG1871	5685	Not in him, but off from him, things are grotesque, eccentric, fail of their full returns,
LG1871	5686	Nothing out of its place is good, nothing in its place is bad,
LG1871	5687	He bestows on every object or quality its fit proportion, neither more nor less,
LG1871	5688	He is the arbiter of the diverse, he is the key,
LG1871	5689	He is the equalizer of his age and land,
LG1871	5690	He supplies what wants supplying—he checks what wants checking,
LG1871	5691	In peace, out of him speaks the spirit of peace, large, rich, thrifty, building populous towns, encour-agingencouraging agriculture, arts, commerce, lighting the study of man, the Soul, health, immortality, gov-ernmentgovernment;
LG1871	5692	In war, he is the best backer of the war—he fetches artillery as good as the engineer's—he can make every word he speaks draw blood;
LG1871	5693	The years straying toward infidelity, he withholds by his steady faith,
LG1871	5694	He is no arguer, he is judgment—(Nature accepts him absolutely;)
LG1871	5695	He judges not as the judge judges, but as the sun fall-ingfalling round a helpless thing;
LG1871	5696	As he sees the farthest, he has the most faith,
LG1871	5697	His thoughts are the hymns of the praise of things,
LG1871	5698	In the dispute on God and eternity he is silent,
LG1871	5699	He sees eternity less like a play with a prologue and denouement,
LG1871	5700	He sees eternity in men and women—he does not see men and women as dreams or dots.
LG1871	5701	For the great Idea, the idea of perfect and free indi-vidualsindividuals,
LG1871	5702	For that idea the bard walks in advance, leader of leaders,
LG1871	5703	The attitude of him cheers up slaves, and horrifies foreign despots.
LG1871	5704	Without extinction is Liberty! without retrograde is Equality!
LG1871	5705	They live in the feelings of young men, and the best women;
LG1871	5706	Not for nothing have the indomitable heads of the earth been always ready to fall for Liberty.
LG1871	5707	For the great Idea!
LG1871	5708	That, O my brethren—that is the mission of Poets.
LG1871	5709	Songs of stern defiance, ever ready,
LG1871	5710	Songs of the rapid arming, and the march,
LG1871	5711	The flag of peace quick-folded, and instead, the flag we know,
LG1871	5712	Warlike flag of the great Idea.
LG1871	5713	(Angry cloth I saw there leaping!
LG1871	5714	I stand again in leaden rain, your flapping folds saluting;
LG1871	5715	I sing you over all, flying, beckoning through the fight —O the hard-contested fight!
LG1871	5716	O the cannons ope their rosy-flashing muzzles! the hurtled balls scream!
LG1871	5717	The battle-front forms amid the smoke—the volleys pour incessant from the line;
LG1871	5718	Hark! the ringing word, Charge!—now the tussle, and the furious maddening yells;
LG1871	5719	Now the corpses tumble curl'd upon the ground,
LG1871	5720	Cold, cold in death, for precious life of you,
LG1871	5721	Angry cloth I saw there leaping.)
LG1871	5722	Are you he who would assume a place to teach, or be a poet here in The States?
LG1871	5723	The place is August—the terms obdurate.
LG1871	5724	Who would assume to teach here, may well prepare himself, body and mind,
LG1871	5725	He may well survey, ponder, arm, fortify, harden, make lithe, himself,
LG1871	5726	He shall surely be question'd beforehand by me with many and stern questions.
LG1871	5727	Who are you, indeed, who would talk or sing to America?
LG1871	5728	Have you studied out the land, its idioms and men?
LG1871	5729	Have you learn'd the physiology, phrenology, politics, geography, pride, freedom, friendship, of the land? its substratums and objects?
LG1871	5730	Have you consider'd the organic compact of the first day of the first year of Independence, sign'd by the Commissioners, ratified by The States, and read by Washington at the head of the army?
LG1871	5731	Have you possess'd yourself of the Federal Constitution?
LG1871	5732	Do you see who have left all feudal processes and poems behind them, and assumed the poems and pro-cessesprocesses of Democracy?

LG1871	5734	Have you sped through fleeting customs, popularities?
LG1871	5735	Can you hold your hand against all seductions, follies, whirls, fierce contentions? are you very strong? are you really of the whole people?
LG1871	5736	Are you not of some coterie? some school or mere religion?
LG1871	5737	Are you done with reviews and criticisms of life? ani-matinganimating now to life itself?
LG1871	5738	Have you vivified yourself from the maternity of These States?
LG1871	5739	Have you too the old, ever-fresh forbearance and im-partialityimpartiality?
LG1871	5740	Do you hold the like love for those hardening to ma-turitymaturity; for the last-born? little and big? and for the errant?
LG1871	5741	What is this you bring my America?
LG1871	5742	Is it uniform with my country?
LG1871	5743	Is it not something that has been better told or done before?
LG1871	5744	Have you not imported this, or the spirit of it, in some ship?
LG1871	5745	Is it not a mere tale? a rhyme? a prettiness? is the good old cause in it?
LG1871	5746	Has it not dangled long at the heels of the poets, politicianspoliticians, literats, of enemies' lands?
LG1871	5747	Does it not assume that what is notoriously gone is still here?
LG1871	5748	Does it answer universal needs? will it improve man-nersmanners?
LG1871	5749	Does it sound, with trumpet-voice, the proud victory of the Union, in that secession war?
LG1871	5750	Can your performance face the open fields and the sea-sidesea-side?
LG1871	5751	Will it absorb into me as I absorb food, air—to appear again in my strength, gait, face?
LG1871	5752	Have real employments contributed to it? original makers—not mere amanuenses?
LG1871	5753	Does it meet modern discoveries, calibers, faces, face to face?
LG1871	5754	What does it mean to me? to American persons, pro-gressesprogresses, cities? Chicago, Kanada, Arkansas? the planter, Yankee, Georgian, native, immigrant, sailors, squatters, old States, new States?
LG1871	5755	Does it encompass all The States, and the unexcep-tionalunexceptional rights of all the men and woman of the earth? (the genital impulse of These States;)

LG1871	5757	Does it see what finally befalls, and has always finally befallen, each temporizer, patcher, outsider, par-tialistpartialist, alarmist, infidel, who has ever ask'd any-thinganything of America?
LG1871	5758	What mocking and scornful negligence?
LG1871	5759	The track strew'd with the dust of skeletons;
LG1871	5760	By the roadside others disdainfully toss'd.
LG1871	5761	Rhymes and rhymers pass away—poems distill'd from foreign poems pass away,

LG1871	5764	America justifies itself, give it time—no disguise can deceive it, or conceal from it—it is impassive enough,

LG1871	5766	If its poets appear, it will in due time advance to meet them—there is no fear of mistake,
LG1871	5767	(The proof of a poet shall be sternly deferr'd, till his country absorbs him as affectionately as he has absorb'd it.)
LG1871	5768	He masters whose spirit masters—he tastes sweetest who results sweetest in the long run;

LG1871	5770	In the need of poems, philosophy, politics, manners, engineering, an appropriate native grand-opera, shipcraft, any craft, he or she is greatest who contributes the greatest original practical ex-ampleexample.
LG1871	5771	Already a nonchalant breed, silently emerging, ap-pearsappears on the streets,

LG1871	5776	Justice, health, self-esteem, clear the way with irresist-ibleirresistible power;
LG1871	5777	How dare you place anything before a man?
LG1871	5778	Fall behind me, States!
LG1871	5779	A man before all—myself, typical, before all.
LG1871	5780	Give me the pay I have served for!
LG1871	5781	Give me to sing the song of the great Idea! take all the rest;
LG1871	5782	I have loved the earth, sun, animals—I have despised riches,
LG1871	5783	I have given alms to every one that ask'd, stood up for the stupid and crazy, devoted my income and labor to others,
LG1871	5784	I have hated tyrants, argued not concerning God, had patience and indulgence toward the people, taken off my hat to nothing known or unknown,
LG1871	5785	I have gone freely with powerful uneducated persons, and with the young, and with the mothers of families,

LG1871	5788	I have claim'd nothing to myself which I have not carefully claim'd for others on the same terms,
LG1871	5789	I have sped to the camps, and comrades found and accepted from every State;
LG1871	5790	(In war of you, as well as peace, my suit is good, Amer-icaAmerica—sadly I boast;
LG1871	5791	Upon this breast has many a dying soldier lean'd, to breathe his last;
LG1871	5792	This arm, this hand, this voice, have nourish'd, rais'd, restored,

LG1871	5796	(Say, O mother! have I not to your thought been faithful?

LG1871	5798	I swear I begin to see the meaning of these things!
LG1871	5799	It is not the earth, it is not America, who is so great,

LG1871	5801	It is to walk rapidly through civilizations, governments, theories,

LG1871	5804	I swear nothing is good to me now that ignores indi-vidualsindividuals,

LG1871	5822	Its power, weapons, testimony, are you and me

LG1871	5828	Freedom, language, poems, employments, are you and me,
LG1871	5829	Past, present, future, are you and me.
LG1871	5830	I swear I dare not shirk any part of myself,
LG1871	5831	Not any part of America, good or bad,
LG1871	5832	Not the promulgation of Liberty—not to cheer up slaves and horrify foreign despots,
LG1871	5833	Not to build for that which builds for mankind,
LG1871	5834	Not to balance ranks, complexions, creeds, and the sexes,

LG1871	5837	I swear I am for those that have never been mas-ter'dmaster'd!
LG1871	5838	For men and women whose tempers have never been master'd,
LG1871	5839	For those whom laws, theories, conventions, can never master.
LG1871	5840	I swear I am for those who walk abreast with the whole earth!
LG1871	5841	Who inaugurate one, to inaugurate all.
LG1871	5842	I swear I will not be outfaced by irrational things!
LG1871	5843	I will penetrate what it is in them that is sarcastic upon me!
LG1871	5844	I will make cities and civilizations defer to me!
LG1871	5845	This is what I have learnt from America—it is the amount—and it I teach again.
LG1871	5846	(Democracy! while weapons were everywhere aim'd at your breast,
LG1871	5847	I saw you serenely give birth to immortal children— saw in dreams your dilating form;
LG1871	5848	Saw you with spreading mantle covering the world.)
LG1871	5849	I will confront these shows of the day and night!
LG1871	5850	I will know if I am to be less than they!
LG1871	5851	I will see if I am not as majestic as they!
LG1871	5852	I will see if I am not as subtle and real as they!
LG1871	5853	I will see if I am to be less generous than they!
LG1871	5854	I will see if I have no meaning, while the houses and ships have meaning!
LG1871	5855	I will see if the fishes and birds are to be enough for themselves, and I am not to be enough for my-selfmyself.

LG1871	5858	America isolated, yet embodying all, what is it finally except myself?
LG1871	5859	These States—what are they except myself?
LG1871	5860	I know now why the earth is gross, tantalizing, wicked—it is for my sake,
LG1871	5861	I take you to be mine, you beautiful, terrible, rude forms.
LG1871	5862	(Mother! bend down, bend close to me your face!
LG1871	5863	I know not what these plots and wars, and deferments are for;
LG1871	5864	I know not fruition's success—but I know that through war and peace your work goes on, and must yet go on.)
LG1871	5865	………Thus, by blue Ontario's shore,
LG1871	5866	While the winds fann'd me, and the waves came troop-ingtrooping toward me,

LG1871	5869	And I saw the free Souls of poets;
LG1871	5870	The loftiest bards of past ages strode before me,

LG1871	5872	O my rapt verse, my call—mock me not!

LG1871	5993	(The war is completed—the price is paid—the title is settled beyond recall;)
LG1871	5994	Let every one answer! let those who sleep be waked! let none evade!
LG1871	5995	Must we still go on with our affectations and sneaking?
LG1871	5996	Let me bring this to a close—I pronounce openly for a new distribution of roles;
LG1871	5997	Let that which stood in front go behind! and let that which was behind advance to the front and speak;
LG1871	5998	Let murderers, bigots, fools, unclean persons, offer new propositions!
LG1871	5999	Let the old propositions be postponed!
LG1871	6000	Let faces and theories be turn'd inside out! let mean-ingsmeanings be freely criminal, as well as results!
LG1871	6001	Let there be no suggestion above the suggestion of drudgery!
LG1871	6002	Let none be pointed toward his destination! (Say! do you know your destination?)
LG1871	6003	Let men and women be mock'd with bodies and mock'd with Souls!
LG1871	6004	Let the love that waits in them, wait! let it die, or pass still-born to other spheres!

LG1871	6006	Let contradictions prevail! let one thing contradict another! and let one line of my poems contra-dictcontradict another!
LG1871	6007	Let the people sprawl with yearning, aimless hands! let their tongues be broken! let their eyes be discouraged! let none descend into their hearts with the fresh lusciousness of love!
LG1871	6008	(Stifled, O days! O lands! in every public and private corruption!

LG1871	6014	Let the crust of hell be near'd and trod on! let the days be darker than the nights! let slumber bring less slumber than waking time brings!
LG1871	6015	Let the world never appear to him or her for whom it was all made!
LG1871	6016	Let the heart of the young man still exile itself from the heart of the old man! and let the heart of the old man be exiled from that of the young man!
LG1871	6017	Let the sun and moon go! let scenery take the applause of the audience! let there be apathy under the stars!
LG1871	6018	Let freedom prove no man's inalienable right! every one who can tyrannize, let him tyrannize to his satisfaction!
LG1871	6019	Let none but infidels be countenanced!
LG1871	6020	Let the eminence of meanness, treachery, sarcasm, hate, greed, indecency, impotence, lust, be taken for granted above all! let writers, judges, govern-mentsgovernments, households, religions, philosophies, take such for granted above all!
LG1871	6021	Let the worst men beget children out of the worst women!

LG1871	6026	Let the cow, the horse, the camel, the garden-bee—let the mud-fish, the lobster, the mussel, eel, the sting-ray, and the grunting pig-fish—let these, and the like of these, be put on a perfect equality with man and woman!

LG1871	6031	Let nothing but copies at second hand be permitted to exist upon the earth!
LG1871	6032	Let the earth desert God, nor let there ever henceforth be mention'd the name of God!
LG1871	6033	Let there be no God!
LG1871	6034	Let there be money, business, imports, exports, custom, authority, precedents, pallor, dyspepsia, smut, ignorance, unbelief!

LG1871	6169	The summer growth is innocent and disdainful above all those strata of sour dead.
LG1871	6170	What chemistry!
LG1871	6171	That the winds are really not infectious,
LG1871	6172	That this is no cheat, this transparent green-wash of the sea, which is so amorous after me,

LG1871	6176	That the cool drink from the well tastes so good,

LG1871	6180	Though probably every spear of grass rises out of what was once a catching disease.
LG1871	6181	Now I am terrified at the Earth! it is that calm and patient,
LG1871	6182	It grows such sweet things out of such corruptions,
LG1871	6183	It turns harmless and stainless on its axis, with such endless successions of diseas'd corpses,
LG1871	6184	It distils such exquisite winds out of such infused fetor,
LG1871	6185	It renews with such unwitting looks, its prodigal, an-nualannual, sumptuous crops,
LG1871	6186	It gives such divine materials to men, and accepts such leavings from them at last.
LG1871	6187	NATIONS ten thousand years before These States, and many times ten thousand years before These States;
LG1871	6188	Garner'd clusters of ages, that men and women like us grew up and travel'd their course, and pass'd on;
LG1871	6189	What vast-built cities—what orderly republics—what pastoral tribes and nomads;
LG1871	6190	What histories, rulers, heroes, perhaps transcending all others;
LG1871	6191	What laws, customs, wealth, arts, traditions;
LG1871	6192	What sort of marriage—what costumes—what physi-ologyphysiology and phrenology;
LG1871	6193	What of liberty and slavery among them—what they thought of death and the soul;
LG1871	6194	Who were witty and wise—who beautiful and poetic— who brutish and undevelop'd;
LG1871	6195	Not a mark, not a record remains—And yet all remains.
LG1871	6196	O I know that those men and women were not for nothing, any more than we are for nothing;
LG1871	6197	I know that they belong to the scheme of the world every bit as much as we now belong to it, and as all will henceforth belong to it.
LG1871	6198	Afar they stand—yet near to me they stand,
LG1871	6199	Some with oval countenances, learn'd and calm,
LG1871	6200	Some naked and savage—Some like huge collections of insects,
LG1871	6201	Some in tents—herdsmen, patriarchs, tribes, horsemen,
LG1871	6202	Some prowling through woods—Some living peaceably on farms, laboring, reaping, filling barns,
LG1871	6203	Some traversing paved avenues, amid temples, palaces, factories, libraries, shows, courts, theatres, won-derfulwonderful monuments.
LG1871	6204	Are those billions of men really gone?
LG1871	6205	Are those women of the old experience of the earth gone?
LG1871	6206	Do their lives, cities, arts, rest only with us?
LG1871	6207	Did they achieve nothing for good, for themselves?
LG1871	6208	I believe of all those billions of men and women that fill'd the unnamed lands; every one exists this hour, here or elsewhere, invisible to us, in exact proportion to what he or she grew from in life, and out of what he or she did, felt, became, loved, sinn'd, in life.
LG1871	6209	I believe that was not the end of those nations, or any person of them, any more than this shall be the end of my nation, or of me;

LG1871	6213	I WAS asking for something specific and perfect for my city,
LG1871	6214	Whereupon, lo! upsprang the aboriginal name!
LG1871	6215	Now I see what there is in a name, a word, liquid, sane, unruly, musical, self-sufficient;

LG1871	6217	Because I see that word nested in nests of water-bays, superb, with tall and wonderful spires,

LG1871	6237	FAR hence, amid an isle of wondrous beauty,
LG1871	6238	Crouching over a grave, an ancient sorrowful mother,
LG1871	6239	Once a queen—now lean and tatter'd, seated on the ground,
LG1871	6240	Her old white hair drooping dishevel'd round her shoul-dersshoulders;

LG1871	6242	Long silent—she too long silent—mourning her shroud-edshrouded hope and heir;

LG1871	6244	Yet a word, ancient mother;
LG1871	6245	You need crouch there no longer on the cold ground, with forehead between your knees;
LG1871	6246	O you need not sit there, veil'd in your old white hair, so dishevel'd;
LG1871	6247	For know you, the one you mourn is not in that grave;
LG1871	6248	It was an illusion—the heir, the son you love, was not really dead;

LG1871	6251	What you wept for, was translated, pass'd from the grave,
LG1871	6252	The winds favor'd, and the sea sail'd it,
LG1871	6253	And now with rosy and new blood,
LG1871	6254	Moves to-day in a new country.
LG1871	6255	TO ORATISTS—to male or female,
LG1871	6256	Vocalism, measure, concentration, determination, and the divine power to use words.
LG1871	6257	Are you full-lung'd and limber-lipp'd from long trial? from vigorous practice? from physique?
LG1871	6258	Do you move in these broad lands as broad as they?
LG1871	6259	Come duly to the divine power to use words?
LG1871	6260	For only at last, after many years—after chastity, friendship, procreation, prudence, and nakedness;
LG1871	6261	After treading ground and breasting river and lake;
LG1871	6262	After a loosen'd throat—after absorbing eras, tempera-mentstemperaments, races—after knowledge, freedom, crimes;
LG1871	6263	After complete faith—after clarifyings, elevations, and removing obstructions;
LG1871	6264	After these, and more, it is just possible there comes to a man, a woman, the divine power to use words.
LG1871	6265	Then toward that man or that woman, swiftly hasten all—None refuse, all attend;

LG1871	6271	Of a great vocalism, the merciless light thereof shall pour, and the storm rage,
LG1871	6272	Every flash shall be a revelation, an insult,
LG1871	6273	The glaring flame on depths, on heights, on suns, on stars,

LG1871	6278	SOLID, ironical, rolling orb!
LG1871	6279	Master of all, and matter of fact!—at last I accept your terms;
LG1871	6280	Bringing to practical, vulgar tests, of all my ideal dreams,

LG1871	6282	BATHED in war's perfume—delicate flag!
LG1871	6283	(Should the days needing armies, needing fleets, come again,)

LG1871	6513	OF Public Opinion;
LG1871	6514	Of a calm and cool fiat, sooner or later, (How impas-siveimpassive! How certain and final!)
LG1871	6515	Of the President with pale face, asking secretly to him-selfhimself, What will the people say at last?
LG1871	6516	Of the frivolous Judge—Of the corrupt Congressman, Governor, Mayor—Of such as these, standing helpless and exposed;
LG1871	6517	Of the mumbling and screaming priest—(soon, soon deserted;)
LG1871	6518	Of the lessening, year by year, of venerableness, and of the dicta of officers, statutes, pulpits, schools;
LG1871	6519	Of the rising forever taller and stronger and broader, of the intuitions of men and women, and of self-esteemself-esteem, and of personality;
LG1871	6520	—Of the New World—Of the Democracies, resplendent, en-masse;
LG1871	6521	Of the conformity of politics, armies, navies, to them and to me,
LG1871	6522	Of the shining sun by them—Of the inherent light, greater than the rest,
LG1871	6523	Of the envelopment of all by them, and of the effusion of all from them.
LG1871	6524	How they are provided for upon the earth, (appearing at intervals;)
LG1871	6525	How dear and dreadful they are to the earth;
LG1871	6526	How they inure to themselves as much as to any— What a paradox appears, their age;
LG1871	6527	How people respond to them, yet know them not;
LG1871	6528	How there is something relentless in their fate, all times;
LG1871	6529	How all times mischoose the objects of their adulation and reward,
LG1871	6530	And how the same inexorable price must still be paid for the same great purchase.
LG1871	6531	STILL, though the one I sing,
LG1871	6532	(One, yet of contradictions made,) I dedicate to Nation-alityNationality,
LG1871	6533	I leave in him Revolt, (O latent right of insurrection! O quenchless, indispensable fire!)
LG1871	6534	COURAGE yet! my brother or my sister!
LG1871	6535	Keep on! Liberty is to be subserv'd, whatever occurs;
LG1871	6536	That is nothing, that is quell'd by one or two failures, or any number of failures,
LG1871	6537	Or by the indifference or ingratitude of the people, or by any unfaithfulness,
LG1871	6538	Or the show of the tushes of power, soldiers, cannon, penal statutes.
LG1871	6539	Revolt! and still revolt! revolt!
LG1871	6540	What we believe in waits latent forever through all the continents, and all the islands and archi-pelagosarchipelagos of the sea;
LG1871	6541	What we believe in invites no one, promises nothing, sits in calmness and light, is positive and com-posedcomposed, knows no discouragement,
LG1871	6542	Waiting patiently, waiting its time.
LG1871	6543	(Not songs of loyalty alone are these,
LG1871	6544	But songs of insurrection also;
LG1871	6545	For I am the sworn poet of every dauntless rebel, the world over,
LG1871	6546	And he going with me leaves peace and routine behind him,
LG1871	6547	And stakes his life, to be lost at any moment.)
LG1871	6548	Revolt! and the downfall of tyrants!
LG1871	6549	The battle rages with many a loud alarm, and frequent advance and retreat,
LG1871	6550	The infidel triumphs—or supposes he triumphs,
LG1871	6551	Then the prison, scaffold, garrote, hand-cuffs, iron neck-lacenecklace and anklet, lead-balls, do their work,
LG1871	6552	The named and unnamed heroes pass to other spheres,
LG1871	6553	The great speakers and writers are exiled—they lie sick in distant lands,
LG1871	6554	The cause is asleep—the strongest throats are still, choked with their own blood,
LG1871	6555	The young men droop their eyelashes toward the ground when they meet;
LG1871	6556	—But for all this, liberty has not gone out of the place, nor the infidel enter'd into full possession,
LG1871	6557	When liberty goes out of a place, it is not the first to go, nor the second or third to go,
LG1871	6558	It waits for all the rest to go—it is the last.
LG1871	6559	When there are no more memories of heroes and martyrs,
LG1871	6560	And when all life, and all the souls of men and women are discharged from any part of the earth,
LG1871	6561	Then only shall liberty, or the idea of liberty, be dis-chargeddischarged from that part of the earth,
LG1871	6562	And the infidel come into full possession.
LG1871	6563	Then courage! European revolter! revoltress!
LG1871	6564	For, till all ceases, neither must you cease.
LG1871	6565	I do not know what you are for, (I do not know what I am for myself, nor what anything is for,)
LG1871	6566	But I will search carefully for it even in being foil'd,
LG1871	6567	In defeat, poverty, misconception, imprisonment—for they too are great.
LG1871	6568	Revolt! and the bullet for tyrants!
LG1871	6569	Did we think victory great?
LG1871	6570	So it is—But now it seems to me, when it cannot be help'd, that defeat is great,
LG1871	6571	And that death and dismay are great.
LG1871	6572	A GREAT year and place;
LG1871	6573	A harsh, discordant, natal scream out-sounding, to touch the mother's heart closer than any yet.
LG1871	6574	I walk'd the shores of my Eastern Sea,
LG1871	6575	Heard over the waves the little voice,
LG1871	6576	Saw the divine infant, where she woke, mournfully wail-ingwailing, amid the roar of cannon, curses, shouts, crash of falling buildings;
LG1871	6577	Was not so sick from the blood in the gutters running —nor from the single corpses, nor those in heaps, nor those borne away in the tumbrils;
LG1871	6578	Was not so desperate at the battues of death—was not so shock'd at the repeated fusillades of the guns.
LG1871	6579	Pale, silent, stern, what could I say to that long-accruedlong-accrued retribution?
LG1871	6580	Could I wish humanity different?
LG1871	6581	Could I wish the people made of wood and stone?
LG1871	6582	Or that there be no justice in destiny or time?
LG1871	6583	O Liberty! O mate for me!
LG1871	6584	Here too the blaze, the grape-shot and the axe, in re-servereserve, to fetch them out in case of need;
LG1871	6585	Here too, though long represt, can never be destroy'd;
LG1871	6586	Here too could rise at last, murdering and extatic;
LG1871	6587	Here too demanding full arrears of vengeance.
LG1871	6588	Hence I sign this salute over the sea,
LG1871	6589	And I do not deny that terrible red birth and baptism,
LG1871	6590	But remember the little voice that I heard wailing—and wait with perfect trust, no matter how long;
LG1871	6591	And from to-day, sad and cogent, I maintain the be-queath'dbequeath'd cause, as for all lands,
LG1871	6592	And I send these words to Paris with my love,
LG1871	6593	And I guess some chansonniers there will understand them,
LG1871	6594	For I guess there is latent music yet in France—floods of it;
LG1871	6595	O I hear already the bustle of instruments—they will soon be drowning all that would interrupt them;
LG1871	6596	O I think the east wind brings a triumphal and free march,
LG1871	6597	It reaches hither—it swells me to joyful madness,
LG1871	6598	I will run transpose it in words, to justify it,
LG1871	6599	I will yet sing a song for you, MA FEMME.
LG1871	6600	SUDDENLY, out of its stale and drowsy lair, the lair of slaves,
LG1871	6601	Like lightning it le'pt forth, half startled at itself,
LG1871	6602	Its feet upon the ashes and the rags—its hands tight to the throats of kings.
LG1871	6603	O hope and faith!
LG1871	6604	O aching close of exiled patriots' lives!
LG1871	6605	O many a sicken'd heart!
LG1871	6606	Turn back unto this day, and make yourselves afresh.
LG1871	6607	And you, paid to defile the People! you liars, mark!
LG1871	6608	Not for numberless agonies, murders, lusts,
LG1871	6609	For court thieving in its manifold mean forms, worming from his simplicity the poor man's wages,
LG1871	6610	For many a promise sworn by royal lips, and broken, and laugh'd at in the breaking,
LG1871	6611	Then in their power, not for all these, did the blows strike revenge, or the heads of the nobles fall;
LG1871	6612	The People scorn'd the ferocity of kings;
LG1871	6613	But the sweetness of mercy brew'd bitter destruction, and the frighten'd monarchs come back;
LG1871	6614	Each comes in state, with his train—hangman, priest, tax-gatherer,
LG1871	6615	Soldier, lawyer, lord, jailer, and sycophant.
LG1871	6616	Yet behind all, lowering, stealing—lo, a Shape,
LG1871	6617	Vague as the night, draped interminably, head, front and form, in scarlet folds,
LG1871	6618	Whose face and eyes none may see,
LG1871	6619	Out of its robes only this—the red robes, lifted by the arm,
LG1871	6620	One finger, crook'd, pointed high over the top, like the head of a snake appears.
LG1871	6621	Meanwhile, corpses lie in new-made graves—bloody corpses of young men,
LG1871	6622	The rope of the gibbet hangs heavily, the bullets of princes are flying, the creatures of power laugh aloud,
LG1871	6623	And all these things bear fruits—and they are good.
LG1871	6624	Those corpses of young men,
LG1871	6625	Those martrys that hang from the gibbets—those hearts pierc'd by the gray lead,
LG1871	6626	Cold and motionless as they seem, live elsewhere with unslaughter'd vitality.
LG1871	6627	They live in other young men, O kings!
LG1871	6628	They live in brothers, again ready to defy you!
LG1871	6629	They were purified by death—they were taught and exalted.
LG1871	6630	Not a grave of the murder'd for freedom, but grows seed for freedom, in its turn to bear seed,
LG1871	6631	Which the winds carry afar and re-sow, and the rains and the snows nourish.
LG1871	6632	Not a disembodied spirit can the weapons of tyrants let loose,
LG1871	6633	But it stalks invisibly over the earth, whispering, coun-selingcounseling, cautioning.
LG1871	6634	Liberty! let others despair of you! I never despair of you.
LG1871	6635	Is the house shut? Is the master away?
LG1871	6636	Nevertheless, be ready—be not weary of watching;
LG1871	6637	He will soon return—his messengers come anon.
LG1871	6638	TO The States, or any one of them, or any city of The States, Resist much, obey little;
LG1871	6639	Once unquestioning obedience, once fully enslaved;
LG1871	6640	Once fully enslaved, no nation, state, city, of this earth, ever afterward resumes its liberty.
LG1871	6641	HERE, take this gift!
LG1871	6642	I was reserving it for some hero, speaker, or General,
LG1871	6643	One who should serve the good old cause, the great Idea, the progress and freedom of the race;
LG1871	6644	Some brave confronter of despots—some daring rebel;
LG1871	6645	—But I see that what I was reserving, belongs to you just as much as to any.
LG1871	6646	WHOEVER you are, I fear you are walking the walks of dreams,
LG1871	6647	I fear these supposed realities are to melt from under your feet and hands;
LG1871	6648	Even now, your features, joys, speech, house, trade, manners, troubles, follies, costume, crimes, dissi-patedissipate away from you,
LG1871	6649	Your true Soul and Body appear before me,
LG1871	6650	They stand forth out of affairs—out of commerce, shops, law, science, work, farms, clothes, the house, medicine, print, buying, selling, eating, drinking, suffering, dying.
LG1871	6651	Whoever you are, now I place my hand upon you, that you be my poem;
LG1871	6652	I whisper with my lips close to your ear,
LG1871	6653	I have loved many women and men, but I love none better than you.
LG1871	6654	O I have been dilatory and dumb;
LG1871	6655	I should have made my way straight to you long ago;
LG1871	6656	I should have blabb'd nothing but you, I should have chanted nothing but you.
LG1871	6657	I will leave all, and come and make the hymns of you;
LG1871	6658	None have understood you, but I understand you;
LG1871	6659	None have done justice to you—you have not done justice to yourself;
LG1871	6660	None but have found you imperfect—I only find no imperfection in you;
LG1871	6661	None but would subordinate you—I only am he who will never consent to subordinate you;
LG1871	6662	I only am he who places over you no master, owner, better, God, beyond what waits intrinsically in yourself.
LG1871	6663	Painters have painted their swarming groups, and the centre figure of all;
LG1871	6664	From the head of the centre figure spreading a nimbus of gold-color'd light;
LG1871	6665	But I paint myriads of heads, but paint no head with-outwithout its nimbus of gold-color'd light;
LG1871	6666	From my hand, from the brain of every man and woman it streams, effulgently flowing forever.
LG1871	6667	O I could sing such grandeurs and glories about you!
LG1871	6668	You have not known what you are—you have slumber'd upon yourself all your life;
LG1871	6669	Your eye-lids have been the same as closed most of the time;
LG1871	6670	What you have done returns already in mockeries;
LG1871	6671	Your thrift, knowledge, prayers, if they do not return in mockeries, what is their return?)
LG1871	6672	The mockeries are not you;
LG1871	6673	Underneath them, and within them, I see you lurk;
LG1871	6674	I pursue you where none else has pursued you;
LG1871	6675	Silence, the desk, the flippant expression, the night, the accustom'd routine, if these conceal you from others, or from yourself, they do not conceal you from me;
LG1871	6676	The shaved face, the unsteady eye, the impure com-plexioncomplexion, if these balk others, they do not balk me,
LG1871	6677	The pert apparel, the deform'd attitude, drunkenness, greed, premature death, all these I part aside.
LG1871	6678	There is no endowment in man or woman that is not tallied in you;
LG1871	6679	There is no virtue, no beauty, in man or woman, but as good is in you;
LG1871	6680	No pluck, no endurance in others, but as good is in you;
LG1871	6681	No pleasure waiting for others, but an equal pleasure waits for you.
LG1871	6682	As for me, I give nothing to any one, except I give the like carefully to you;
LG1871	6683	I sing the songs of the glory of none, not God, sooner than I sing the songs of the glory of you.
LG1871	6684	Whoever you are! claim your own at any hazard!
LG1871	6685	These shows of the east and west are tame, compared to you;
LG1871	6686	These immense meadows—these interminable rivers— you are immense and interminable as they;
LG1871	6687	These furies, elements, storms, motions of Nature, throes of apparent dissolution—you are he or she who is master or mistress over them,
LG1871	6688	Master or mistress in your own right over Nature, ele-mentselements, pain, passion, dissolution.
LG1871	6689	The hopples fall from your ankles—you find an un-failingunfailing sufficiency;
LG1871	6690	Old or young, male or female, rude, low, rejected by the rest, whatever you are promulges itself;
LG1871	6691	Through birth, life, death, burial, the means are pro-videdprovided, nothing is scanted;
LG1871	6692	Through angers, losses, ambition, ignorance, ennui, what you are picks its way.
LG1871	6693	As the time draws nigh, glooming, a cloud,
LG1871	6694	A dread beyond, of I know not what, darkens me.
LG1871	6695	I shall go forth,
LG1871	6696	I shall traverse The States awhile—but I cannot tell whither or how long;
LG1871	6697	Perhaps soon, some day or night while I am singing, my voice will suddenly cease.
LG1871	6698	O book, O chants! must all then amount to but this?
LG1871	6699	Must we barely arrive at this beginning of us?… And yet it is enough, O soul!
LG1871	6700	O soul! we have positively appear'd—that is enough.
LG1871	6701	YEARS of the modern! years of the unperform'd!
LG1871	6702	Your horizon rises—I see it parting away for more august dramas;
LG1871	6703	I see not America only—I see not only Liberty's nation, but other nations preparing;
LG1871	6704	I see tremendous entrances and exits—I see new com-binationscombinations—I see the solidarity of races;
LG1871	6705	I see that force advancing with irresistible power on the world's stage;
LG1871	6706	(Have the old forces, the old wars, played their parts? are the acts suitable to them closed?)
LG1871	6707	I see Freedom, completely arm'd, and victorious, and very haughty, with Law on one side, and Peace on the other,
LG1871	6708	A stupendous Trio, all issuing forth against the idea of caste;
LG1871	6709	—What historic denouements are these we so rapidly approach?
LG1871	6710	I see men marching and countermarching by swift mil-lionsmillions;
LG1871	6711	I see the frontiers and boundaries of the old aristocracies broken;
LG1871	6712	I see the landmarks of European kings removed;
LG1871	6713	I see this day the People beginning their landmarks, (all others give way;)
LG1871	6714	—Never were such sharp questions ask'd as this day;
LG1871	6715	Never was average man, his soul, more energetic, more like a God;
LG1871	6716	Lo! how he urges and urges, leaving the masses no rest;
LG1871	6717	His daring foot is on land and sea everywhere—he col-onizescolonizes the Pacific, the archipelagoes;

LG1871	6721	Are all nations communing? is there going to be but one heart to the globe?
LG1871	6722	Is humanity forming, en-masse?—for lo! tyrants trem-bletremble, crowns grow dim;
LG1871	6723	The earth, restive, confronts a new era, perhaps a gen-eralgeneral divine war;
LG1871	6724	No one knows what will happen next—such portents fill the days and nights;
LG1871	6725	Years prophetical! the space ahead as I walk, as I vain-lyvainly try to pierce it, is full of phantoms;
LG1871	6726	Unborn deeds, things soon to be, project their shapes around me;
LG1871	6727	This incredible rush and heat—this strange extatic fever of dreams, O years!
LG1871	6728	Your dreams, O years, how they penetrate through me! (I know not whether I sleep or wake!)
LG1871	6729	The perform'd America and Europe grow dim, retiring in shadow behind me,
LG1871	6730	The unperform'd, more gigantic than ever, advance, ad-vanceadvance upon me.
LG1871	6731	OF these years I sing,
LG1871	6732	How they pass and have pass'd, through convuls'd pains, as through parturitions;
LG1871	6733	How America illustrates birth, muscular youth, the promise, the sure fulfillment, the Absolute Suc-cessSuccess, despite of people—Illustrates evil as well as good;
LG1871	6734	How many hold despairingly yet to the models de-parteddeparted, caste, myths, obedience, compulsion, and to infidelity;

LG1871	6737	How the great cities appear—How the Democratic masses, turbulent, wilful, as I love them;

LG1871	6740	How America is the continent of glories, and of the triumph of freedom, and of the Democracies, and of the fruits of society, and of all that is begun;
LG1871	6741	And how The States are complete in themselves—And how all triumphs and glories are complete in themselves, to lead onward,

LG1871	6745	OF seeds dropping into the ground—of birth,
LG1871	6746	Of the steady concentration of America, inland, upward, to impregnable and swarming places,

LG1871	6767	(O it lurks in me night and day—What is gain, after all, to savageness and freedom?)
LG1871	6768	SPLENDOR of ended day, floating and filling me!
LG1871	6769	Hour prophetic—hour resuming the past!
LG1871	6770	Inflating my throat—you, divine average!
LG1871	6771	You, Earth and Life, till the last ray gleams, I sing.
LG1871	6772	Open mouth of my Soul, uttering gladness,
LG1871	6773	Eyes of my Soul, seeing perfection,
LG1871	6774	Natural life of me, faithfully praising things;
LG1871	6775	Corroborating forever the triumph of things.
LG1871	6776	Illustrious every one!
LG1871	6777	Illustrious what we name space—sphere of unnum-ber'dunnumber'd spirits;

LG1871	6780	Illustrious the passing light! Illustrious the pale reflection on the new moon in the western sky!
LG1871	6781	Illustrious whatever I see, or hear, or touch, to the last.
LG1871	6782	Good in all,
LG1871	6783	In the satisfaction and aplomb of animals,
LG1871	6784	In the annual return of the seasons,
LG1871	6785	In the hilarity of youth,
LG1871	6786	In the strength and flush of manhood,
LG1871	6787	In the grandeur and exquisiteness of old age,
LG1871	6788	In the superb vistas of Death.
LG1871	6789	Wonderful to depart;
LG1871	6790	Wonderful to be here!
LG1871	6791	The heart, to jet the all-alike and innocent blood!
LG1871	6792	To breathe the air, how delicious!
LG1871	6793	To speak! to walk! to seize something by the hand!
LG1871	6794	To prepare for sleep, for bed—to look on my rose-color'drose-color'd flesh;

LG1871	6796	To be this incredible God I am;
LG1871	6797	To have gone forth among other Gods—these men and women I love.
LG1871	6798	Wonderful how I celebrate you and myself!
LG1871	6799	How my thoughts play subtly at the spectacles around!
LG1871	6800	How the clouds pass silently overhead!
LG1871	6801	How the earth darts on and on! and how the sun, moon, stars, dart on and on!
LG1871	6802	How the water sports and sings! (Surely it is alive!)
LG1871	6803	How the trees rise and stand up—with strong trunks— with branches and leaves!
LG1871	6804	(Surely there is something more in each of the trees— some living Soul,)
LG1871	6805	O amazement of things! even the least particle!
LG1871	6806	O spirituality of things!
LG1871	6807	O strain musical, flowing through ages and continents —now reaching me and America!
LG1871	6808	I take your strong chords—I intersperse them, and cheerfully pass them forward.
LG1871	6809	I too carol the sun, usher'd, or at noon, or, as now, setting,
LG1871	6810	I too throb to the brain and beauty of the earth, and of all the growths of the earth,
LG1871	6811	I too have felt the resistless call of myself.
LG1871	6812	As I sail'd down the Mississippi,
LG1871	6813	As I wander'd over the prairies,
LG1871	6814	As I have lived—As I have look'd through my windows, my eyes,
LG1871	6815	As I went forth in the morning—As I beheld the light breaking in the east;
LG1871	6816	As I bathed on the beach of the Eastern Sea, and again on the beach of the Western Sea;

LG1871	6818	Or cities, or silent woods, or peace, or even amid the sights of war;
LG1871	6819	Wherever I have been, I have charged myself with con-tentmentcontentment and triumph.
LG1871	6820	I sing the Equalities, modern or old,
LG1871	6821	I sing the endless finales of things;
LG1871	6822	I say Nature continues—Glory continues;
LG1871	6823	I praise with electric voice;
LG1871	6824	For I do not see one imperfection in the universe;
LG1871	6825	And I do not see one cause or result lamentable at last in the universe.
LG1871	6826	O setting sun! though the time has come,
LG1871	6827	I still warble under you, if none else does, unmitigated adoration.
LG1871	6828	WHEN I heard the learn'd astronomer;
LG1871	6829	When the proofs, the figures, were ranged in columns before me;
LG1871	6830	When I was shown the charts and the diagrams, to add, divide, and measure them;
LG1871	6831	When I, sitting, heard the astronomer, where he lec-turedlectured with much applause in the lecture-room,
LG1871	6832	How soon, unaccountable, I became tired and sick;
LG1871	6833	Till rising and gliding out, I wander'd off by myself,
LG1871	6834	In the mystical moist night-air, and from time to time,
LG1871	6835	Look'd up in perfect silence at the stars.
LG1871	6836	WHAT you give me, I cheerfully accept,
LG1871	6837	A little sustenance, a hut and garden, a little money— these, as I rendezvous with my poems;
LG1871	6838	A traveler's lodging and breakfast as I journey through The States—Why should I be ashamed to own such gifts? Why to advertise for them?
LG1871	6839	For I myself am not one who bestows nothing upon man and woman;
LG1871	6840	For I bestow upon any man or woman the entrance to all the gifts of the universe.
LG1871	6841	OF what I write from myself—As if that were not the resumé;
LG1871	6842	Of Histories—As if such, however complete, were not less complete than the preceding poems;
LG1871	6843	As if those shreds, the records of nations, could possibly be as lasting as the preceding poems;
LG1871	6844	As if here were not the amount of all nations, and of all the lives of heroes.
LG1871	6845	TO conclude—I announce what comes after me;
LG1871	6846	I announce mightier offspring, orators, days, and then, for the present, depart.
LG1871	6847	I remember I said, before my leaves sprang at all,
LG1871	6848	I would raise my voice jocund and strong, with reference to consummations.
LG1871	6849	When America does what was promis'd,
LG1871	6850	When there are plentiful athletic bards, inland and seaboard,
LG1871	6851	When through These States walk a hundred millions of superb persons,
LG1871	6852	When the rest part away for superb persons, and con-tributecontribute to them,
LG1871	6853	When breeds of the most perfect mothers denote America,
LG1871	6854	Then to me and mine our due fruition.
LG1871	6855	I have press'd through in my own right,
LG1871	6856	I have sung the Body and the Soul—War and Peace have I sung,
LG1871	6857	And the songs of Life and of Birth—and shown that there are many births:
LG1871	6858	I have offer'd my style to every one—I have journey'd with confident step;
LG1871	6859	While my pleasure is yet at the full, I whisper, So long!
LG1871	6860	And take the young woman's hand, and the young man's hand, for the last time.
LG1871	6861	I announce natural persons to arise;
LG1871	6862	I announce justice triumphant;
LG1871	6863	I announce uncompromising liberty and equality;
LG1871	6864	I announce the justification of candor, and the justifica-tionjustification of pride.
LG1871	6865	I announce that the identity of These States is a single identity only;
LG1871	6866	I announce the Union more and more compact, indis-solubleindissoluble;
LG1871	6867	I announce splendors and majesties to make all the previous politics of the earth insignificant.
LG1871	6868	I announce adhesiveness—I say it shall be limitless, unloosen'd;
LG1871	6869	I say you shall yet find the friend you were looking for.
LG1871	6870	I announce a man or woman coming—perhaps you are the one, (So long!)
LG1871	6871	I announce the great individual, fluid as Nature, chaste, affectionate, compassionate, fully armed.
LG1871	6872	I announce a life that shall be copious, vehement, spiritual, bold;
LG1871	6873	I announce an end that shall lightly and joyfully meet its translation;
LG1871	6874	I announce myriads of youths, beautiful, gigantic, sweet-bloodedsweet-blooded;
LG1871	6875	I announce a race of splendid and savage old men.
LG1871	6876	O thicker and faster! (So long!)
LG1871	6877	O crowding too close upon me;
LG1871	6878	I foresee too much—it means more than I thought;
LG1871	6879	It appears to me I am dying.
LG1871	6880	Hasten throat, and sound your last!
LG1871	6881	Salute me—salute the days once more. Peal the old cry once more.
LG1871	6882	Screaming electric, the atmosphere using,
LG1871	6883	At random glancing, each as I notice absorbing,
LG1871	6884	Swiftly on, but a little while alighting,
LG1871	6885	Curious envelop'd messages delivering,
LG1871	6886	Sparkles hot, seed ethereal, down in the dirt dropping,
LG1871	6887	Myself unknowing, my commission obeying, to question it never daring,
LG1871	6888	To ages, and ages yet, the growth of the seed leaving,
LG1871	6889	To troops out of me, out of the army, the war arising— they the tasks I have set promulging,
LG1871	6890	To women certain whispers of myself bequeathing— their affection me more clearly explaining,
LG1871	6891	To young men my problems offering—no dallier I—I the muscle of their brains trying,
LG1871	6892	So I pass—a little time vocal, visible, contrary;
LG1871	6893	Afterward, a melodious echo, passionately bent for— (death making me really undying;)
LG1871	6894	The best of me then when no longer visible—for toward that I have been incessantly preparing.
LG1871	6895	What is there more, that I lag and pause, and crouch extended with unshut mouth?
LG1871	6896	Is there a single final farewell?
LG1871	6897	My songs cease—I abandon them;
LG1871	6898	From behind the screen where I hid, I advance person-allypersonally, solely to you.
LG1871	6899	Camerado! This is no book;
LG1871	6900	Who touches this, touches a man;
LG1871	6901	(Is it night? Are we here alone?)
LG1871	6902	It is I you hold, and who holds you;
LG1871	6903	I spring from the pages into your arms—decease calls me forth.
LG1871	6904	O how your fingers drowse me!
LG1871	6905	Your breath falls around me like dew—your pulse lulls the tympans of my ears;
LG1871	6906	I feel immerged from head to foot;
LG1871	6907	Delicious—enough.
LG1871	6908	Enough, O deed impromptu and secret!
LG1871	6909	Enough, O gliding present! Enough, O summ'd-up past!
LG1871	6910	Dear friend, whoever you are, take this kiss,
LG1871	6911	I give it especially to you—Do not forget me;
LG1871	6912	I feel like one who has done work for the day, to retire awhile;
LG1871	6913	I receive now again of my many translations—from my avataras ascending—while others doubtless await me;
LG1871	6914	An unknown sphere, more real than I dream'd, more direct, darts awakening rays about me—So long!
LG1871	6915	Remember my words—I may again return,
LG1871	6916	I love you—I depart from materials;
LG1871	6917	I am as one disembodied, triumphant, dead.
